# PianoLIT code review — 2026-09-22

## Scope and limits

Repository-wide static checks and targeted manual review covered PHP, route registration, Blade templates, JavaScript, SCSS imports/build configuration, model relationships, search/feeds, favorites, authentication, billing/webhooks, file delivery, and maintenance/test infrastructure. The starting inventory included 415 application PHP files, 68 route files, 62 JavaScript files, 53 stylesheet files, and 908 Blade templates.

This is not a claim that every behavior or integration has been exercised. No production database, Redis cleanup, billing action, email campaign, or deployment was run. The full legacy suite, real mobile clients, browser visual flows, and MySQL-specific behavior remain unverified. Working UI/design and mobile response formats were preserved; SCSS required no source changes after compilation checks.

## Implemented fixes

| Area | Evidence and change | Verification |
| --- | --- | --- |
| Gift downloads | `UsersController::gift` accepted arbitrary paths relative to `public`, including traversal outside it. Resolve canonical paths and allow only public/static gifts and public-storage gifts; reject symlink escapes. Missing fallback files return 404. | Traversal, symlink, invalid input, and valid-file regression checks using temporary fixtures. |
| Stripe webhooks | Signature verification was commented out. Verify the raw request body using the installed Stripe SDK, configured secret and tolerance; reject invalid signatures, fail closed without a secret, and acknowledge signed unhandled event types. Updated the legacy webhook test helper to sign requests. | Valid signature, forged/unsigned/tampered/expired payloads, and missing-secret tests. Real billing lifecycle was not exercised. |
| Restricted API token | Missing bearer/config tokens compared equal; direct `env()` use was incompatible with config caching. Use config and a nonempty constant-time comparison. | Missing, incorrect, and matching token tests. |
| Favorites | Moving a favorite deleted its source before destination validation. Wrap the move in a transaction. | Successful move, duplicate destination rollback, foreign-folder rollback. |
| Favorite folders | Foreign/missing folder reads dereferenced null. PDF generation lacked a web ownership check. Form lookups used `firstOrFail` incorrectly, body fields could override the route's ownership target, and unchanged names failed uniqueness validation. Reorder input was not constrained to an array of IDs in the folder. | Ownership, route/body mismatch, missing folder, unchanged name/description, malformed reorder, and PDF authorization tests. |
| Folder performance | `hasPiece` ran an extra query per folder even when favorites were already loaded. Reuse that relation and batch-load piece favorites for flat folder lists. | Zero-query assertion for loaded membership checks. |
| Recommendations | Calls supplied named argument `order` while the scope accepts `ordered`. Use a positional boolean compatible with the declared PHP range. | Empty-favorites recommendation query runs successfully. |
| Search | Request-controlled classes could be instantiated; malformed search/filter input and missing local matches could crash. Validate input, allow only the existing tag/composer models, and handle empty/missing matches. | Invalid model/search/filter/page cases, empty filtered search, empty count, missing tag tests. |
| Feed caches | Uninitialized Redis version keys could collapse different caches onto an empty key. Personalized/platform rows could mutate an in-memory cached collection. Add distinct fallback keys and copy collections before insertion. | Cache separation, repeated discover calls, and web/mobile explore isolation tests. |
| Empty catalog | No free pick caused null dereferences while creating free/similar rows. Return empty row content; tolerate absent explore highlight. | Empty free/similar row tests. |
| Admin autocomplete | Request `field` was interpolated into raw SQL. Restrict it to fields used by the UI; select distinct scalar rows without hydrating full Piece models and relations. | SQL-expression rejection test; source caller review. |
| Artisan startup | `RefreshDailyLogsCount` scanned Redis from its constructor during unrelated commands. Defer that scan until the command executes. | All isolated database migrations execute with Redis calls forbidden unless explicitly mocked. |
| Browser helpers | First-cookie reads included subsequent cookies, malformed escapes could throw, and writes appended `undefined`. Query parameter values did not encode `&`/`=` correctly. | Cookie and URL encoding checks in `npm run test:js`. |
| Audio | `audio.pause` was referenced rather than invoked; clearing the source assigned `null`. Correct stop/reload and reset icons on end/error/rejected playback. | Mock audio lifecycle checks. |
| Async search/autocomplete | Stale failures could erase current results or hide a newer spinner. Lookup's timeout called autocomplete immediately. Cancel obsolete searches, guard all callbacks by request ID, debounce lookup correctly, and respond to input/paste. | Out-of-order success/error, repeated query, clear-input, cancellation, and debounce checks. |

Compiled `public/js/app.js`, `public/js/admin.js`, the copied audio/lookup scripts, and the two bundle manifest entries were updated from the successful isolated production build. Stylesheet outputs were not changed.

## Validation

- Initial syntax scan: 659 non-Blade PHP files passed.
- All 62 JavaScript source files passed `node --check`.
- All 908 Blade templates compiled and their generated PHP passed syntax checks.
- All six SCSS entry points and three JS entry points compiled in the production build; Mix's copy/concatenation/versioning steps completed. Initial temporary-build attempts needed corrections to the test harness's symlink paths and Laravel detection; no production build configuration changes were needed.
- Isolated regression suite: 33 tests, 55 assertions passing.
- JavaScript regression runner passed; no new dependencies were installed.
- Route reflection audit: 455 routes, with 16 missing action targets listed below.
- No measured end-to-end latency claim is made. The query reduction is verified narrowly, and frontend request reduction follows from tested debounce/cancellation behavior.

## Follow-up register

### P0 — Mobile authentication requires a coordinated rollout

`app/Http/Middleware/AttemptLoginAppUser.php` logs in whichever account is identified by a caller-supplied `user_id`. `Auth/Api/LoginController` returns user data after password checking but issues no verifiable API credential. Protected data and mutations must use authenticated tokens and derive ownership from the authenticated principal. Coordinate server and mobile releases, token issuance/revocation, existing-client migration, and negative authorization tests. Retrofitting mandatory tokens here without mobile changes would break existing clients. This serious issue remains open; the folder fixes are not a replacement for authentication.

### P1 — Complete webhook reliability and integration checks

- Before deploying the signature change, confirm `STRIPE_WEBHOOK_SECRET` matches the actual endpoint. There is one configured signing secret; review the US/Swiss billing setup and whether separate endpoints/secrets are needed.
- `StripeWebhooks::whenChargeSucceeded` inserts a payment on every delivery; `payments.charge_id` has no unique index. Design idempotency using Stripe event/charge IDs, audit existing duplicates before introducing uniqueness, and test concurrent retries/out-of-order events with real schema constraints.
- `MailgunWebhook` uses the mail API secret and a 15-second acceptance window. Verify the actual signing configuration and delivery/retry behavior before changing it; add malformed-input and signature tests. No live webhook delivery was tested.

### P1 — Isolate and restore the broader test suite

The new review suite is safe for local in-memory checks. Original tests use `Tests/AppTest`, real Redis-oriented setup/cleanup, and Stripe sandbox calls. Establish a disposable integration environment, replace shell Redis cleanup with the configured connection, fake outbound services by default, and explicitly group integration tests. Add CI for PHP tests, JS regressions, syntax/template checks, and asset builds. Then baseline failures before a framework upgrade.

### P1 — Resolve missing route actions without guessing intended behavior

These registered routes refer to nonexistent methods. They were left unchanged because the intended replacement/removal and external callers need review. Confirm access logs and client use; implement supported behavior or retire the route, then add a route-action integrity check.

| Route | Missing action |
| --- | --- |
| `GET ebooks/topics/{topic}` | `Shop\eBooksController@topic` |
| `GET escores/topics/{topic}` | `Shop\eScoresController@topic` |
| `test-upload` | `HomeController@filetest` |
| Subscription resource: index, create, show, update, destroy | `SubscriptionsController` methods |
| `admin/blog/images/remove` | `Admin\BlogController@removeImage` |
| `admin/ebooks/{ebook}` | `Admin\eBooksController@show` |
| `admin/escores/{escore}` | `Admin\eScoresController@show` |
| `admin/pieces/datatable` | `PiecesController@datatable` |
| `admin/quiz/images/remove` | `Admin\QuizzesController@removeImage` |
| `admin/tutorial-requests/{tutorialRequest}` | `Admin\TutorialRequestsController@show` |
| `admin/pieces/{piece}` | `Admin\PiecesController@show` |
| `admin/playlists/create` | `Admin\PlaylistsController@create` |

### P2 — Serialization, search correctness, and query budgets

`Piece::$appends` computes many attributes, including relationship and sibling queries. Review explicit API resources and bounded eager loading with mobile payload snapshots before changing it. Favorites relations can include other users' favorite metadata; define and test a minimal public payload. `Api\Search` applies Scout filters after retrieval/pagination, while count requests bypass them; plan consistent filtering/count/pagination against representative Algolia data. Current fixes deliberately preserve the existing response shape. Profile large composer/playlists feeds and `ORDER BY RAND()` paths before replacing them.

### P2 — Redis maintenance and concurrency

`FlushRedis` and `RefreshDailyLogsCount::flushAll` invoke `redis-cli` through a shell rather than honoring the configured Redis connection. Other maintenance code uses `KEYS`. Replace these with bounded `SCAN` batches and configured connection operations, validating namespaces and testing empty/multiple pages. Review favorite toggle races and nullable-folder uniqueness with the actual MySQL version; the move transaction does not solve simultaneous duplicate inserts.

### P2 — Staged dependency and frontend modernization

Inventory Composer/npm advisories and plan framework/PHP, Mix/Webpack/node-sass, jQuery/Bootstrap, and Axios updates in tested stages. No dependency security audit or updates were performed in this pass. Introduce bundle-size baselines and measure page-specific loading before splitting bundles. Keep Bootstrap/Font Awesome/SCSS ordering stable; visual smoke tests are needed before CSS consolidation or switching Sass compilers.

### P2 — Integration and asset edge cases

Review direct Guzzle calls (Apple receipts and reCAPTCHA) for bounded timeouts, response validation, and provider-specific retry/fallback rules. Review runtime `env()` calls outside config, registration/upload CSRF exceptions, admin upload validation, and source-file exposure on the web server. The gift endpoint's legacy default JPEG is absent in this local checkout; invalid/missing gifts now return 404 if that fallback is unavailable. Confirm the deployed gift inventory.

## Future entries

Add a date, issue reference, affected paths, evidence, implemented change, verification, and any rollout dependency. Keep unresolved findings visible until the relevant change and verification are complete.

### 2026-09-30 — Single Bootstrap 5 website build

- P1 frontend compatibility: `package.json` installed Bootstrap 4.6.2 for the shared public/admin bundles alongside a Bootstrap 5.3.8 alias used by the save panel. The compiled public bundles consequently contained both releases.
- Replaced the two dependencies with `bootstrap@5.3.8`, loaded its Popper-inclusive bundle for public, `my.*`, and admin, and reused its offcanvas constructor. Updated Bootstrap component data attributes and selectors across Blade/JavaScript, plus renamed removed spacing, alignment, grid, and form utilities. `App\Support\Icon` now emits Bootstrap 5 logical spacing classes while keeping its caller options stable.
- Adapted the project's button Sass mixin call, removed the hand-copied offcanvas CSS, and added a small local stylesheet for existing file-input wrappers, form groups, input-group wrappers, and icon close buttons. Kept the admin radio/checkbox button group state working with a change handler. Removed unreferenced `public/js/{app,admin} 2.js` and `public/css/{app,admin} 2.css` plus `public/css/app 3.css`, which still contained Bootstrap 4.6.2.
- Initial verification: `npm ls bootstrap` reports only 5.3.8; `npm run production` compiled all entry points and refreshed tracked assets and the Mix manifest; `npm run test:js` passed; the isolated PHP suite passed 123 tests/5,519 assertions; `git diff --check` passed. Visual regressions reported after that build are addressed in the follow-up below. Mobile JSON routes were not changed.

### 2026-09-30 — Restore appearance after the Bootstrap 5 migration

- P1 reported rendering regression: production's css-loader 0.28/cssnano 3 longhand optimizer consumed `--bs-border-width` and `--bs-border-style`, replacing them with an actual red `:root` border. Browser computed styles confirmed missing component borders. A minimal cssnano reproduction confirmed that disabling `mergeLonghand` preserves the variables.
- Affected files: `webpack.mix.js`, the shared Bootstrap theme and compatibility Sass, custom corners/buttons/border colors, responsive Sass, the favorites menu alignment, `package.json`, `tests/css/regressions.js`, compiled CSS/JS assets and the Mix manifest. The production CSS loader now disables that optimizer. Every production build verifies all nonempty Bootstrap CSS variable names from unminified app/admin Sass against the shipped CSS and checks the root border definitions.
- Restored the original container widths, 30px grid gutters, compact form gutters, card/list padding, button group dimensions, alert colors and spacing, native select arrows/heights, label spacing, upload and input-group joins, table rules/header shading, pill badges, side corners, link decoration, and close-button placement. Adjusted custom breakpoint mixin calls to preserve their original width ranges under Bootstrap 5 semantics. Bootstrap 5.3.8 remains the sole framework dependency; no routes, content-access checks, mobile contracts, or database changes.
- Verification: production build and its CSS gate pass (437 Bootstrap variables preserved in each bundle); JS regressions pass; isolated PHP review suite passes 123 tests/5,515 assertions on repeat. The first PHP run hit an existing timestamp-sensitive collections assertion (`updated_at` advanced by one second), unrelated to CSS. `git diff --check` passes. Local public home, guest Discover/piece Score tab and admin login were inspected in the browser; the score loaded and tab selection worked. Original/current app and admin component fixtures were compared with computed styles and screenshots. Static public/piece snapshots were checked at 390px and 800px alongside the live desktop layout. Live 390px public navigation/dropdowns and piece Score controls also worked without document overflow; snapshots alone cannot verify the score editor's canvas resizing or animated tab positioning.
- Remaining work: deploy sources, rebuilt assets and manifest together. Authenticated admin widgets and account-only favorites/upload submissions were not exercised in the browser; no production deployment or physical mobile-client test was performed.

### 2026-09-30 — Replace custom side panels with native Bootstrap offcanvas

- P2 requested component migration: `#options-panel` and admin `#notifications-panel` used a hand-built overlay, inline positioning, jQuery fades and a separate body scroll-lock class. Both now use Bootstrap 5 `offcanvas-end`, native headers/bodies, accessible titles, `data-bs-toggle`/`data-bs-target` triggers and `data-bs-dismiss` close controls. Side panels retain a 420px width capped by the viewport. Notification links, read actions and footer content are preserved.
- Affected files: piece Options/header/favorite and admin notifications/header Blade templates, app/admin JS entry points, the new shared offcanvas event integration, favorites/modals/triggers JS, Bootstrap theme/global/admin Sass, focused PHP/JS regressions, compiled assets and the Mix manifest. Removed the obsolete custom overlay partial, panel Sass and fixed-panel animation/scroll-lock handlers. The Options Share and Manage favorites actions are keyboard-accessible buttons.
- Integration: Bootstrap owns animation, backdrop, Escape handling, focus trapping and scrolling. When an offcanvas hands off to another offcanvas or modal, the shared integration waits for Bootstrap's `hidden.bs.offcanvas` event before opening the target, then restores focus to the visible original opener when the target closes. Save to retains its loaded folder content during this transition, avoiding a second fetch. Routes, authentication/ownership, mobile JSON contracts and stored data are unchanged.
- Verification: production build and CSS variable gate pass; JS regressions cover overlay handoffs, scrolling ownership, focus restoration and a single folder fetch during the Save to transition. The isolated PHP suite passes 124 tests/5,542 assertions, including guest/account Options markup and authenticated admin notifications markup. The live guest piece page was inspected at 390px and 1280px: native opening, width, focus trap, X/Escape/backdrop dismissal, restored scrolling and opener focus worked. A local fixture using the compiled production bundle and a mocked folder response verified Options → Share and Options → Save to, their backdrops and focus restoration. `git diff --check` passes.
- Remaining work: deploy Blade, rebuilt CSS/JS and manifest together. The account-only browser transitions used a fixture; real notification read/favorite writes and production deployment were not performed.

### 2026-09-28 — Current free pick fully open on the web app

- P1 correction: the paid/trial/grace/super-user content check ignored the current free-pick flag. The admin highlight action already clears the previous `is_free` flag before setting it on the new piece.
- Added `Piece::hasWebMediaAccess()` in `PieceStatus`, combining the existing `is_free` flag with the established account subscription override. The piece-page composer, AJAX audio/tutorial fragments, score downloads, and authenticated annotation controller use that same check. Tutorial fragments now receive their route-bound piece. Downloads use the public storage disk where piece scores are stored in every environment.
- Guests get full audio, top-down and Synthesia playback, and a readable score with page/zoom/fullscreen/download/print controls for the current free pick. The existing score editor skips annotation requests and disables/hides marking tools for guests; authenticated non-paying accounts can save their own free-pick markings. Favorites and annotation writes retain session authentication. Removing `is_free` restores all ordinary content restrictions on the next request, including annotation authorization. Mobile API contracts and original public URLs are unchanged.
- Verification: 85 isolated PHP tests / 1,235 assertions and JS regressions passed. Regression coverage includes guest/non-paying free-pick access, denied guest favorites/annotation writes, flag removal, ordinary-piece forged IDs, registered markings during the free-pick period, and guest score loading without account requests or autosave. The isolated Mix build passed; the published score-editor script and its manifest hash are updated. A browser fixture verified a clear score, enabled zoom/print/download controls, hidden marking tools, and no annotation-load error. Live billing, production deployment, and actual media streaming were not exercised.

### 2026-09-22 — Full web access through subscription grace periods

- Request: preserve full content access during grace periods, including trials canceled before their end date.
- Updated the existing web `hasActiveSubscription()` check to honor Stripe's `isOnGracePeriod()` whenever a scheduled membership end exists. That end date takes precedence over a leftover renewal date, so cancellation preserves access until the scheduled end without extending it afterward. An actually ended subscription remains restricted. Mobile contracts and cancellation/billing actions are unchanged.
- Verification: 50 isolated PHP tests / 541 assertions and JavaScript regressions passed. Tests cover full scores/downloads, audio and videos during active/trial/canceled/paused grace periods, restriction after grace expires even with a future renewal date, and immediate-ended subscriptions. No live billing calls or deployment were performed.

### 2026-09-22 — Full web content access during active trials

- Correction: active trials must have the same full content access as paying subscribers. The previous paid-only predicate explicitly excluded trials.
- Renamed the web content predicate to `hasActiveSubscription()` and reused the billing source's `isActive()` with the existing expiry/end checks. Applied it to the piece view, AJAX audio/tutorial fragments, and score-download controller. Stripe trial states and Apple trial periods now receive readable scores and unrestricted media. Paused, ended, expired, and unpaid subscriptions remain restricted. Mobile API and billing behavior are unchanged.
- Verification: 49 isolated PHP tests / 422 assertions and JavaScript regressions passed. Coverage includes paid Stripe/Apple accounts, Stripe `trialing`/`trial`, Apple seven-day trials, full-score downloads, untagged audio/video playback, and expired/paused/ended trials. No live billing calls or deployment were performed.

### 2026-09-22 — Piece content access rules (implemented locally)

- Request: keep piece pages public while limiting visitor/non-paying content; the user chose 10-second previews. This replaces the temporary unrestricted media policy below. The free-pick flag does not bypass these new web content rules.
- Inspection: route middleware controls page entry; `HasMembership` and Stripe/Apple billing sources control subscription status; `webapp/piece/index.blade.php` initializes Plyr and AJAX/native audio; the score tab uses PDF.js or a Safari embed. There was no existing preview limiter/modal, only the Go Premium pricing flow. The implementation reuses those players, billing methods, Bootstrap modal component, button styles, and pricing destination.
- Added `hasActivePaidSubscription()` to the existing membership trait, using source paid/expired/ended/trial checks. Content templates receive a separate `hasMediaAccess` flag; the established `isAuthorized()` method and mobile API remain unchanged. Trials, paused/expired/ended subscriptions, and accounts without a paid subscription receive previews. No synthetic subscription is created for visitors or privileged accounts.
- `config/webapp.php` sets the 10-second limit. The page-specific `piece-access.js` handles playback, seeking, replay attempts, early endings, fullscreen exit, and AJAX-loaded players. Paid players have no limit attributes/listeners. All audio variants and tutorial fragments use the same rules. The tutorial endpoint also validates that a tutorial belongs to its piece.
- Restricted scores render every PDF page sequentially inside one blurred container, including on Safari. Download/share controls and native PDF embeds are absent for restricted viewers; the Laravel score-download endpoint also checks paid access. Existing paid score rendering/download controls are retained. Corrected the old PDF render-state variable typo and skipped PDF initialization when no viewer exists.
- Verification: 49 isolated PHP tests / 350 assertions passed, plus JavaScript regressions for cutoff/seeking/replay, subscriber passthrough, dynamic audio, multi-page rendering, and load errors. Isolated Mix asset build succeeded; the copied page script is included in `public/js/views`. Browser fixture checks verified the upgrade prompt, AJAX audio stopping at 10 seconds, Synthesia seeking clamped to 10 seconds, three-page blur, subscriber playback beyond 10 seconds, and readable subscriber scores. These use synthetic local media, not production billing or content. One native audio-control interaction crashed the test browser; the check succeeded through the existing hand-selection controls after reopening the fixture. Mobile Safari and production CDN behavior still require deployment smoke checks.
- Delivery limitation / follow-up: original videos/audio/PDFs remain publicly hosted and the browser must fetch originals for playback/PDF rendering. The UI restrictions and protected Laravel download route do not prevent direct URL access or developer-tools bypass. Strong file-level enforcement requires private/signed originals, real 10-second preview files, and server-generated blurred page images; coordinate that work with the separately deployed media service and mobile clients. No storage permissions or remote media were changed, and no production deployment was performed.

### 2026-09-22 — Recently viewed repertoire (implemented locally)

- Request: show Recently viewed directly above Latest pieces on Discover, only for registered users after opening a piece.
- Added account-scoped history through `app/Services/RecentlyViewedPieces.php` and a `recently_viewed_pieces` migration. Successful GETs of the piece detail page update one unique account/piece row using an atomic upsert. Indexed reads return the latest 12 distinct pieces, with repeat visits moving a piece forward. Foreign keys remove history when an account or piece is deleted.
- `WebApp/PiecesController` records visits using the authenticated session; `WebApp/TabsController` inserts the row outside the shared discovery cache using the existing gallery/card templates. Guests, HEAD requests, and piece subresource requests do not create history. Mobile API feeds are unchanged.
- Verification: four isolated feature tests cover first visit, placement, repeat ordering, account isolation/forged IDs, guest exclusion, cache/API isolation, card limit, and deletion cleanup. Full isolated suite: 44 tests / 192 assertions passed. No live database or visual browser check was performed; no assets changed.
- Rollout: run `2026_09_22_120000_create_recently_viewed_pieces_table.php` before serving the updated controllers. The migration was exercised only against disposable SQLite; production MySQL migration/deployment remains pending. History starts with visits after rollout; old activity logs are not backfilled.

Main-domain mobile API authentication remains the P0 follow-up above; this change does not repair that broader vulnerability or verify which hosts deployed mobile clients use.

- Verification: isolated PHP suite passes 40 tests / 161 assertions, including 7 new guest/session access regressions covering public pages, premium content, denied writes, forged identity, folder ownership/reordering, and applause. JavaScript regression runner passes. No production deployment, live billing/upload integration, or browser visual verification was performed.
- Remaining: define the next content limits. The preexisting `pieces/{piece}/appleMusic` route renders a missing `webapp.piece.options.apple-music` template and has no current webapp template caller; confirm intended behavior before restoring or retiring it. It is excluded from the passing public-page smoke checks.


### 2026-09-23 — Visitor search limit and webapp query reduction (implemented locally)

- Priority: P1 behavior change and P2 performance. Visitors now receive at most the first three matching search results, followed by Sign up / Sign in links. The server rejects subsequent visitor result pages with an empty response, ignores caller-supplied identity for access, and applies local filters before limiting. Scout filters scan backend pages in relevance order until three matches are found. Registered accounts, including accounts without a subscription, retain all results and pagination. The mobile `Search::get()` behavior remains separate.
- Evidence and fixes: piece-card templates previously performed separate video, performance, Synthesia, and favorite lookups per piece. `Services/WebApp/PieceCards.php` batches tags/composers and uses database EXISTS aggregates for card status. Discover clones cached piece models before adding browser attributes; personalized rows and favorite status are not shared. Explore/highlights/Synthesia releases and favorite-folder contents load tags in batches. The Discover composer picker no longer loads all composers' pieces, and unused post/category queries are removed.
- Piece detail loads tutorials once and reuses them for media/category checks, computes similar pieces once, and passes the already-bound Piece to Timeline. Timeline no longer loads unused countries. Playlist completeness reuses tutorial counts; the browser playlist index computes qualifying piece counts without hydrating the entire repertoire. Save-to responses aggregate per-folder saved status, and My Pieces reuses its folder collection/counts. Profile renders reuse membership and newsletter-list relationships.
- The webapp's browser bootstrap serializes only the identity fields it uses, avoiding incidental favorite/media/activity serialization on every signed-in page. Search count reuses one resolved search, exact-match middleware passes its resolved model forward, and browser pagination omits the unused database total count. The search client stops visitor scrolling, prevents overlapping scroll requests, ignores stale filter responses, and restores controls after failures.
- Affected files: `app/Api/{Api,Factory,Search}.php`, `app/Services/WebApp/PieceCards.php`, webapp controllers, `Http/Middleware/Search/CheckForLocalQueries.php`, `Piece`, `Playlist`, `Timeline`, `Subscription`, membership/media traits, webapp card/search/feed/account templates, the shared newsletter-preferences template and favorite-response controller, and new isolated PHP/JavaScript regressions.
- Query measurement: before/after measurements used the same disposable SQLite fixture (12 pieces with tags/tutorials, six folders, one playlist); cache was cleared for each measured route. Activity logging/geolocation middleware and external services were excluded. Counts are SQL statements, not latency measurements; cold Discover can vary slightly with randomized rows. Representative results:

  | Page | Before | After |
  | --- | ---: | ---: |
  | Discover, visitor / registered | 163 / 250 | 39 / 49 |
  | Explore | 23 | 11 |
  | Highlights results | 15 | 4 |
  | Playlist index, visitor / registered | 21 / 20 | 5 / 4 |
  | Playlist detail, visitor / registered | 41 / 53 | 6 / 6 |
  | Piece detail, visitor / registered | 70 / 73 | 15 / 18 |
  | Similar pieces, visitor / registered | 42 / 53 | 10 / 10 |
  | Search results, visitor / registered | 41 / 41 | 7 / 7 |
  | Search count | 5 | 3 |
  | My Pieces, registered | 89 | 11 |
  | Profile, registered without subscription | 5 | 2 |
  | Save-to, six folders | 10 | 4 |

- Verification: the full isolated PHP suite passed (66 tests / 836 assertions), JavaScript regressions passed, and `git diff --check` passed. No JS/SCSS bundle sources changed; browser script changes are rendered by Blade. Isolated PHP regressions cover route query budgets, constant card query counts for 2 versus 12 pieces, subscriber media payload parity, batched Synthesia tags, cache/account isolation, profile newsletter/membership reads, save/remove feedback, and playlist count parity. Search tests cover visitor limits, page/identity/count bypass attempts, filters, empty searches, free registered pagination, unchanged mobile result counts, and mocked Scout pagination. JavaScript tests cover duplicate requests, stopping visitor scrolling, stale responses, retries, and empty responses. The signup fragment was visually checked in a local browser fixture with the existing stylesheet.
- Compatibility and remaining verification: no new schema migration, asset bundle, storage policy, mobile payload, or membership-access change is required for this work. Existing media/history changes retain their previously documented rollout requirements. No production deployment or production query profiling was performed; MySQL execution plans, real search-service latency, and production-size data still need deployment smoke checks. This is a measured reduction of demonstrated redundant queries, not proof of a globally minimal query count. The preexisting missing appleMusic view remains tracked separately.

### 2026-09-23 — Restore super-user full access (implemented and verified locally)

- `app/Traits/HasMembership.php` now honors `super_user` before inspecting subscription records. This restores unrestricted piece video, Synthesia, audio variants, tutorial fragments, readable scores, and score downloads even without a subscription or with an expired/ended historical subscription. Billing records and mobile contracts are unchanged. `AGENTS.md` now explicitly preserves this existing entitlement.
- Verification: the new regression in `tests/Review/PieceContentAccessTest.php` failed before the fix and passes afterward. It covers no subscription, ended/expired Stripe, expired Apple, all media/download endpoints, and restoring normal restrictions when the override is removed. ### 2026-09-23 — Persistent web score annotations (implemented locally)

- Priority: P1 requested feature. Full-access accounts, including unconditional super-user overrides, now get a PDF.js score editor with pen strokes, fingering/text, colors, eraser, undo/redo, page navigation, and zoom. Normalized coordinates keep markings aligned when the page changes size. Guest/non-paying score previews and copyrighted-score purchase links retain their existing behavior.
- Persistence: `score_annotations` has a unique account/piece/PDF key, validated bounded annotation data, cascading cleanup, and revision checks. Session-authenticated web endpoints derive identity from the session and route, bind the current score path and PDF fingerprint, and return private/no-store responses. Another piece using the same PDF still has separate markings. Transactional saves reject stale revisions, roll back failures, and acknowledge identical retries after a lost response. No original PDF or mobile API is modified; downloads remain the original PDF.
- Browser behavior: the editor starts only when its Score tab is visible, loads saved annotations once, debounces writes, serializes in-flight saves, and retains unsaved changes on request failure. Save status, retry controls, conflict feedback, and a navigation warning make failures visible. Editing waits for the saved state to load. A normal subsequent annotation save performs one annotation UPDATE (measured in the isolated test); no duplicate insert or read is needed. The initial save may insert the row, and a conflict/retry reads it to resolve the revision.
- Affected files: `WebApp/ScoreAnnotationsController`, `routes/webapp/pieces.php`, the score-table migration, piece index/score/editor Blade templates, `resources/js/views/score-editor.js`, score-editor SCSS/app import, Mix configuration and relevant tracked assets/manifest, and PHP/JavaScript regressions. `AGENTS.md` records the identity and rollout rules.
- Verification: isolated PHP suite passes 76 tests / 1,066 assertions; JavaScript regressions and isolated production asset build pass. New tests cover account/piece/PDF isolation, session identity, access rules (including super users), validation, persistence/deletion, idempotent retries, stale writes, transaction rollback, and query count. JavaScript tests cover undo/redo, page coordinates, save serialization, failed/lost responses, and conflicts. Browser checks used the actual Blade editor and PDF.js against a local stub persistence service: pen/text saved and survived reopening, another piece remained blank, eraser/undo worked, zoom preserved alignment, and the 390px layout remained usable. Real Laravel endpoints were exercised separately by the isolated PHP tests.
- Rollout and remaining verification: deploy `2026_09_23_120000_create_score_annotations_table.php` before serving the new editor. No production migration or deployment was performed. SQLite tests do not verify production MySQL concurrency/execution; real-device iPad/Apple Pencil and production storage/CORS smoke checks remain. Annotation export into a modified PDF is not part of this implementation.

### 2026-09-23 — Write score text directly on the page (implemented locally)

- Priority: P2 usability. Removed the separate toolbar text input. Selecting Fingering / text and clicking the PDF now focuses a native text field at that point, with the normal blinking caret and live text. Existing text can be clicked to edit. Enter or clicking elsewhere finishes editing; an empty draft disappears without creating an annotation, and clearing existing text removes it.
- Text still autosaves while typing. Edits within one typing session share one undo checkpoint, avoiding an undo action for every character. The temporary field uses the score's normalized coordinates, font/color/size, and measured text baseline so it stays aligned with the saved SVG text through resizing and zoom. Page changes finish the current edit. No endpoint, schema, membership, PDF-delivery, or mobile-contract changes were needed.

Added behavior checks for blank drafts, autosaving active text, editing without duplication, removal, and grouped undo. A local browser fixture verified the caret and live text on the page, alignment when finishing an edit, reopening/editing saved text, no extra save for a blank new entry, and clearing an existing annotation. Native keyboard deletion was verified. Production deployment and real-device keyboard testing remain pending.

### 2026-09-23 — Keep Text active when reopening a saved mark (implemented locally)

Clicking saved text keeps Text active and permits editing; clicking outside still closes the input and returns to Read. The current score template has no “click existing text to edit” instruction after the prior simplification.

### 2026-09-27 — Unwind nested piece navigation with browser Back (implemented locally)

- Priority: P2 navigation defect. The shared arrow used `url()->previous()` as a new link destination, so opening another piece and returning could create a two-page loop. Repeated clicks unwind piece → composer → piece paths to their original search page without adding history entries. The decision to use Back is saved in that history entry and preserves unrelated history state, so returning/reloading a new tab's first page still uses its fallback.

Direct visits, missing/external referrers, and new tabs fall back to Discover; option-page fallbacks retain their route-bound piece destination. Modified clicks keep normal link behavior. Appearance, routes, authentication, media access, PDF delivery, and mobile contracts are unchanged. Preserved the unrelated Match tour/layout changes and copied only this task's relevant build outputs.
- Verification: JavaScript regressions cover nested navigation without growing history, original search query/page, new-tab first entries after return/reload, empty/external/invalid referrers, unrelated history state, modified clicks, and unavailable `replaceState`. A real local browser fixture unwound four levels to the exact search URL, including a reload mid-return, and verified a direct first-entry fallback after returning/reloading. Relevant isolated PHP tests pass (14 tests / 648 assertions); isolated production asset build and `git diff --check` pass.
- Remaining work: deploy the Blade/app assets together and smoke-check actual production navigation, including mobile Safari. Browser verification used a local fixture rather than the production database/site. No deployment performed.

Forward/reload restored Timeline; opening a nested piece and selecting Score returned to the original Timeline page, then home. ### 2026-09-28 - Move administration to its subdomain (implemented locally)

Impersonation explicitly signs into the web guard and returns to the configured public website.

Preserved unrelated API, Explore/grid, and Finder metadata edits.
- Verification: 16 focused isolated PHP tests pass with 3,443 assertions, covering every registered admin route and HTTP method on production/local domains, route priority, public/mobile route matching, preview URLs, legacy links and query encoding, guest/web-user restrictions, valid/invalid login, intended destinations, logout, authenticated piece-creation rendering, impersonation's target/guard, and token-required image uploads using fake storage. ### 2026-10-01 — Add video Moments listening guides (implemented locally)

- Priority: P3 requested listening-guide feature. The piece page uses CDN Plyr 3.7.8 rather than an npm Plyr dependency; its native `markers.points` API supports decimal positions and marker clicks. Library recordings live in `tutorials`, with separate records for performance, synthesia, and lessons. New `video_moments` rows belong to a tutorial and store millisecond-precision start/end seconds, title, plain-text commentary, display order, and timestamps; deleting a tutorial cascades its moments.
- Web integration: performance and synthesia players, plus the existing AJAX lesson endpoint, receive explicit escaped guide data. Plyr supplies the progress markers; a native title attribute also labels fractional markers because 3.7.8's seek tooltip rounds time. The native API excludes zero, so a moment at zero uses epsilon for marker placement and seeks to exactly zero on activation. Native marker keyboard access is added without replacing the progress bar. Moments lists format M:SS/H:MM:SS and seek/play the same instance. Playback/seek events update the active row and an optional commentary control; opening commentary keeps playback running, closes with X/Escape/outside click, and works within fullscreen. No moments means no guide markup or controls.
- Timing: explicit endpoints are inclusive. Missing ends use `config/webapp.php`'s eight-second window, bounded by the next start; overlaps choose the latest eligible start, with admin order breaking equal-start ties. Small guide players place the native seek bar on its own control row and shorten the volume slider while retaining all controls. Commentary scrolls above the controls on narrow players. Guide rows remain outside the embedded player's height cap; players without guides retain the existing About stretch behavior. CSS outside the new scoped feature rules is preserved byte for byte in the shipped app stylesheet.
- Administration: existing saved video fields in the piece editor link to a small Moments form using the existing admin layout and piece update policy. Seconds, M:SS, and H:MM:SS accept up to three decimal places. Administrators can add/edit/delete/reorder rows; validated saves preserve IDs, reject cross-video IDs, run transactionally, and lock the parent video with a revision hash to reject stale tabs. Both routes use the admin subdomain, session guard, and web CSRF middleware. New videos can receive moments after saving the piece.
- Lifecycle correction: the existing lesson loader could initialize an undefined selector or remove permanent piece players when switching lessons. It now initializes actual video elements, ignores stale AJAX responses, restores failed-loading controls, and removes only its dynamically inserted lesson wrapper. Guide document handlers and player references are explicitly cleaned up, including before Plyr is ready. Plyr 3.7.8 emits `destroyed` on its restored original element, so cleanup listens there rather than on the discarded player container.
- Affected files: new migration/model/admin controller and Moments editor partials/scripts; `Tutorial`, web `PiecesController`, admin piece routes/video-field partial, shared video component, piece performance/synthesia/AJAX templates and initializer; scoped Sass, webapp config, Mix registration, tracked standalone JS/CSS/manifest, and focused PHP/JavaScript regressions.
- Compatibility: existing paid/trial/grace/super-user/free-pick rules and ten-second previews remain in effect, including seeking through guide rows. Guide relations are hidden from `Tutorial` serialization, retaining the mobile payload shape even when web code eager-loads moments. Public video/PDF URLs and score annotations are unchanged. Guides do not protect direct-file access. No dependency was added.
- Verification: migration up/rollback through isolated SQLite tests; focused Moments, media-access, and query-budget checks passed (28 tests, 1,013 assertions). Coverage includes per-video ownership, empty guides, escaped text, timestamp parsing, ordering/stable IDs, add/edit/delete, stale revisions, rollback after partial writes, admin authorization/CSRF, video deletion cascade, and unchanged tutorial serialization. JavaScript/CSS regressions, isolated production/development builds, changed PHP/JavaScript syntax, asset hashes/source copies, and `git diff --check` passed. Full review suite ran 141 tests / 5,817 assertions with only the previously documented unrelated collections-heading assertion failure. SQLite-rendered browser fixtures with a generated 90-second local video verified list seek/play, exact native marker seeking (including decimals), keyboard skipping, active rows, non-pausing commentary, close behavior, fullscreen, desktop/390px/320px layouts, and guest guide seeking clamped to 10 seconds with the premium prompt. Admin browser checks verified adding, timestamp entry, and reordering/reindexing.
- Remaining work: deploy `2026_10_01_120000_create_video_moments_table.php` before serving the new templates/controller, then ship PHP/Blade and rebuilt assets/manifest together and refresh cached views if used. No normal-environment migration, production deployment, production-catalog playback, physical iOS/native fullscreen, or MySQL concurrency test was performed. The existing collections-heading assertion remains separate follow-up work. The current guide feature applies to library `Tutorial` videos; user-uploaded community `Performance` records have no guide data-entry interface.

### 2026-10-01 — Mask admin moment times and prevent overlapping saves (implemented locally)

- Priority: P3 requested data-entry improvements. Start/end inputs reuse the admin's installed Inputmask 4.0.9 for MM:SS with an automatic colon, optional millisecond fractions, and explicit longer minute values such as 120:00. Existing stored seconds/hour timestamps render in minute/second format without rounding away fractions. The first added moment starts at 00:00; later additions copy the last displayed row's valid end time exactly. If that end is blank/incomplete, the new start stays blank.
- Validation: the editor displays an accessible overlap alert and marks the affected time fields, blocking Save. Validation follows native edits plus Inputmask's own keyboard/completion/clearing callbacks; fixing, deleting, and reordering rows refreshes the result. The server also sorts ranges by start and rejects overlaps/duplicate starts before any database writes, regardless of submitted display order. Shared end/start boundaries are valid. Existing optional-end playback behavior remains bounded by the next start, so those shortened windows do not create an overlap. Existing overlapping records remain viewable but must be corrected before saving the editor.
- Affected files: admin Moments controller, `VideoMoment::formatTimeInput`, editor/row Blade templates, standalone admin guide JavaScript and copied output/Mix manifest, focused PHP and new JavaScript regressions. No dependencies, schema, mobile payloads, web player styles/timing, or access rules changed. Legacy server seconds/H:MM:SS input remains accepted; the browser presents the requested masked format.
- Verification: focused guide, piece-access, and web-query review tests passed (31 tests / 1,034 assertions), including timestamp precision, chronological/nested overlaps, duplicate starts, adjacent boundaries, optional ends, rollback, ownership, authorization/CSRF, and revision conflicts. JavaScript regressions, PHP/JavaScript syntax, isolated production build/CSS checks, copied source/version hash checks, and `git diff --check` passed. ### 2026-10-01 — Compact moment editor with chronological order and second steppers (implemented locally)

- Priority: P3 requested admin usability follow-up. Each moment now uses a compact card with start/end/title on one desktop row, a two-line resizable commentary field, and a small accessible delete icon. Move up/down controls are removed. Cards measure about 177px high in the desktop fixture and 223px at 390px, where the title stacks below the two time fields.
- Ordering: the editor and saved-preview list show existing records chronologically. The browser sorts after leaving a row or performing an action/submitting, retaining the same DOM fields and focus instead of jumping while typing. Saves independently sort validated times and assign sequential sort_order values, preserving moment IDs and input order for equal starts. The revision hash is calculated before display sorting so old manually ordered data does not cause a false stale-tab rejection. New moments inherit the last chronological row's end time.
- Time controls: paired chevrons and captured ArrowUp/ArrowDown keys change masked timestamps by 1,000 milliseconds using Inputmask's setvalue API. They preserve fractional seconds, carry across minutes, and stay within storage bounds; start cannot become negative and end decrements stop at the start. An empty end steps from the start. Incomplete typed values remain available for correction. Existing masks, optional ends, overlap precedence, preview playback, authorization/CSRF, transactions, and stale-tab protection remain.
- Affected files: admin Moments controller, row/form Blade, standalone admin JavaScript/output, new scoped admin Sass partial/import and compiled CSS, Mix manifest, and focused PHP/JavaScript regressions. No schema/dependency change. Existing admin CSS before the appended editor rules is preserved byte for byte.
- Verification: focused guide, piece-access, and web-query review checks passed (33 tests / 1,070 assertions); stable IDs, chronological save/display ordering, equal-start ties, legacy revisions, and existing ownership/rollback checks are covered. JavaScript/CSS regressions, isolated production build, syntax, source parity/version hashes, and git diff --check passed. Browser fixtures verified chevrons and keyboard stepping, 00:59.125→01:00.125 rollover, complete rows staying together when moved, chronological prefill, and a 390px layout without horizontal overflow. Screenshot: `/tmp/pianolit-moments-compact-editor.jpg`.

### 2026-10-01 — Keep iPhone video playback inline with Moments (implemented locally)

- Priority: P2 user reported iPhone playback automatically entering the system video fullscreen view and losing the Moments overlay. The shared video element lacked playsinline. Plyr 3.7.8 has playsinline enabled in config but its HTML5 setup does not insert that attribute; WebKit requires it on the actual element. System video fullscreen cannot include the guide's HTML overlay.
- Affected files: resources/views/components/video.blade.php now includes playsinline and the legacy webkit-playsinline attribute, plus a focused VideoMomentsTest regression. This applies to piece, tutorial response, and admin preview videos with or without moments. Existing Plyr defaults retain fullscreen.enabled/fallback true and iosNative false, allowing fullscreen of the player container or its built-in full-window fallback without replacing controls. No new fullscreen implementation, JavaScript/CSS build, migration, dependencies, mobile API, or preview-access changes.
- Evidence: WebKit inline-playback documentation at https://webkit.org/blog/6784/new-video-policies-for-ios/ and version-specific Plyr sources at https://github.com/sampotts/plyr/tree/v3.7.8/src/js (defaults, media/html5, fullscreen).
- Verification: isolated VideoMomentsTest and PieceContentAccessTest passed (23 tests / 801 assertions), including inline attributes on every rendered video across piece/tutorial/admin, empty/populated guides, and retained preview rules. PHP syntax and git diff --check passed. A SQLite-rendered browser fixture confirmed both attributes, About remaining hidden before playback, showing after Play, and staying visible in fullscreen of the player container. Screenshot: /tmp/pianolit-inline-fullscreen-moments.png.
- Remaining work: deploy the Blade change and refresh cached views if used. The automatic iPhone behavior still needs physical Safari/iPhone verification; desktop browser testing does not reproduce the iOS system player. An embedded browser that forbids inline playback may also require its host application's inline-media setting. No production deployment performed.

### 2026-10-01 — Curated piece timelines for admin and web (implemented locally)

- Historical record: the per-piece curation implementation described here was retired on 2026-10-02. See the shared-library cleanup entry below for the current architecture; these old models, routes and admin assets are no longer active.

- Priority: P2 requested feature. The old `timelines` table contains shared global events; `App\Timeline::for()` is also called by the iOS-facing controller. It has no piece ownership, descriptions, or image attribution, so it is unsuitable for piece-specific curation without changing legacy mobile behavior. It remains unchanged. The piece schema already stores optional `composed_in` and `published_in` years.
- Behavior: Edit timeline links from the existing admin piece editor to a dedicated page showing piece/composer context. The reference year starts blank and is entered manually. Find 10 events / Find 10 more discover candidates; individual Save actions copy snapshots into the piece's saved timeline. Saved events can be edited, dated precisely or by year, removed, and given a replacement HTTPS image URL with editable attribution. Existing piece/composer media use model-specific cropping/storage handlers, so this feature uses thumbnail URLs and does not add a new uploader. Public timeline rows use only saved database content, automatically add the current piece when a usable composition/publication year exists (composition takes precedence), and sort chronologically. Only Timeline content changes; page header, navigation, tabs, access rules and other content retain their behavior.
- Created files: `app/PieceTimelineEvent.php`, `app/Http/Controllers/Admin/PieceTimelineController.php`, `app/Services/Timeline/{WikimediaDiscovery,WebTimeline}.php`, `config/wikimedia.php`, `database/migrations/2026_10_01_120000_create_piece_timeline_events_table.php`, `resources/views/admin/pages/pieces/timeline/{edit,candidates,saved}.blade.php`, `resources/views/webapp/piece/components/timeline.blade.php`, `resources/js/views/piece-timeline-admin.js` and copied public output, `resources/sass/components/_piece-timeline.scss`, `tests/Review/PieceTimelineTest.php`, and `tests/js/piece-timeline-admin.js`.
- Changed files: `app/Piece.php` adds a relationship without adding eager loads/appends; `app/Http/Controllers/WebApp/PiecesController.php` selects the new web service; `routes/admin/pieces.php` adds admin-session/CSRF-protected routes and discovery throttling; the admin piece edit Blade adds the link; web timeline tab/event/options Blade and the piece page's old timeline-only inline CSS are updated; `resources/sass/app.scss`, `webpack.mix.js`, `public/css/app.css`, `public/mix-manifest.json`, and `tests/js/regressions.js` include the scoped assets/checks. This register records the implementation.
- Database: the new table stores `piece_id`, year, optional exact date, curated title/description, thumbnail URL, image file source/credit/license links, Wikipedia source, Wikidata ID, stable event identifier/kind, text attribution and timestamps. A foreign key cascades piece deletion; a unique `(piece_id, source_id)` constraint prevents concurrent duplicate saves. Global timeline schema/data are preserved. Dates respect Wikidata precision: a stored January 1 with year precision is not presented as an exact date.
- Official endpoints: `https://query.wikidata.org/sparql` discovers dated items; `https://en.wikipedia.org/w/api.php` uses `extracts|pageimages|info` for plain-text introductions, free thumbnails and article information; `https://commons.wikimedia.org/w/api.php` uses `imageinfo` for thumbnail URLs and attribution/license metadata. No HTML scraping. Commons filename normalization/redirects are resolved. Requests identify PianoLIT using the configured User-Agent, with a 3-second connection limit, 20-second SPARQL timeout and 8-second enrichment timeouts. Optional summary/image failures preserve Wikidata candidates; uncredited images are omitted. Discovery failures return a readable retry message. The public page fetches image URLs in the browser, with a text-only fallback if an image fails; it makes no Wikimedia backend API requests.
- Selection/ranking: independently bounded pools (up to 50 records for each of inception P571, publication P577, event date P585, birth P569 and death P570) require an English Wikipedia article and at least 15 sitelinks. Music, art, literature, science and historical events are recognized from Wikidata classes/occupations and English descriptions. General births/deaths require at least 80 sitelinks; battle/siege/skirmish/appointment titles are excluded. Ranking combines category priority, three points per year of distance, a modest birth/death penalty and a capped sitelink bonus. The surrounding range progresses through ±3, ±7, ±15, ±25 and ±40 years as new candidates run out; at most two new ranges are queried per discovery request. The quality/notability/category rules are heuristics, so admin curation remains necessary. Sparse/exhausted periods may return fewer than ten candidates with an explicit status.
- Duplicate/session behavior: stable keys combine Wikidata item, milestone kind and source year. Inception/publication for the same work/year collapse to one event. Saved IDs and every previously shown candidate are excluded server-side. Per-search UUIDs keep browser searches separate; session snapshots expire after two hours and retain up to six searches. Saving accepts only a candidate actually returned for that piece/session and does not trust submitted content or URLs. Editing the displayed date/title preserves the original identifier. The unique database index handles simultaneous saves; repeat saves return the existing curated record. Browser controls serialize discovery/save actions and recover after failures.
- Compatibility: no `routes/api`, API controller, resource/serializer, `App\Timeline`, or global timeline table changes. The legacy mobile timeline result is compared before/after web curation in regressions. Guest browsing and existing content-access rules remain intact. The new relationship is never automatically loaded into mobile serialization. No dependencies, new framework, live public discovery, or normal-environment maintenance commands were introduced.
- Verification: 39 focused PHP checks passed (1,160 assertions), covering admin guard/manual reference year, pagination/exclusion, ranking, individual/idempotent saves, cross-piece ownership, forged candidates, validation, safe rendering, updates/removal, cascade deletion, year precision, filename normalization, image credits, optional/upstream failure recovery, legacy mobile output and public rendering without HTTP. JavaScript checks cover search cursors, CSRF, retry/control recovery, save serialization/duplicate clicks and expired searches. Isolated production build including CSS checks passed; compiled existing app CSS preceding the new timeline rules is unchanged. Browser Blade fixtures at 1200px and 390px verified desktop/mobile timelines with real Wikimedia thumbnails, highlighted piece, image-free events, no overflow, and the existing admin layout with a blank reference year. A live 1730 ±3 query returned 62 ranked candidates, including Haydn's birth, Cristofori's death and Bach's Magnificat; all ten enriched candidates received credited thumbnails after filename normalization.
- Remaining work: deploy the migration **before** the new admin/web controllers/views, and deploy sources/build outputs/manifest together (refresh compiled views if used). No migration or deployment was run against the normal environment. MySQL migration/concurrency behavior, production endpoint availability and physical-device behavior remain unverified. The final isolated review suite passed 155 of 156 tests (6,072 assertions), with one unchanged collection wording failure. An earlier run also exposed unrelated collection assertions (expected “The PianoLit path” while the existing view says “The PianoLit series”, plus a time-bound `updated_at` comparison); these files and their behavior were left unchanged.

### 2026-10-01 — Include world context in timeline discovery (implemented locally)

- Priority: P3 requested discovery refinement. Historical events were permitted but narrowly recognized and ranked behind culture. Discovery now recognizes wars, treaties, rebellions, revolutions, exploration, independence, major disasters and other historical milestones, using direct Wikidata classes and English description heuristics. A sixth bounded date pool uses P580 (start time), alongside the existing inception/publication/point-in-time/birth/death pools. Each batch reserves up to three places for the best available world events, then fills remaining places by the existing cultural/date/notability ranking. Missing world candidates do not reduce batch size. World events require at least 30 sitelinks; battles/sieges require 80, while appointments/skirmishes remain excluded. These remain curation heuristics, not guarantees of historical significance or availability for every period.
- Affected files: `app/Services/Timeline/WikimediaDiscovery.php`, admin `PieceTimelineController`, focused `PieceTimelineTest`, and this register. Pool cache version increments to avoid reusing earlier culture-heavy discovery pools. Only selected IDs are removed from the session pool, preserving skipped cultural candidates for later batches. Wikidata/year/milestone identity supplements source-ID exclusions, so the same event discovered via an inception/start/date claim is not reoffered when already shown or saved under an older identifier. Births and deaths stay distinct. Ranking metadata is removed before storing curated content. The existing ±10 initial range, chronological Before/In/After presentation, public database-only timeline, database schema and iOS-facing API are unchanged. No asset changes are required for this follow-up.
- Verification: focused isolated PHP suite passed (10 tests / 109 assertions), covering mixed first/later batches, retention of skipped candidates, exhausted world candidates with full cultural fallback, duplicate date claims, older saved identifiers, direct historical class recognition, minor-event filtering and prior admin/public/mobile behavior. PHP syntax and git diff --check passed. A live official Wikidata query for 1730 ±10 years was attempted with the isolated review configuration; it timed out, so actual upstream coverage was not verified. No normal-environment database writes, cache maintenance or migrations were performed.
- Remaining work: deploy the service/controller together and start a new search to obtain the revised mix. Existing open searches keep their already fetched pools until exhausted. No production deployment performed.

### 2026-10-01 — Reliable Wikimedia discovery and balanced dates (implemented locally)

- Priority: P2 demonstrated discovery timeout and P3 requested date balance. Reproducing the former combined query for 1730 ±10 produced cURL error 28 after 20.003 seconds, HTTP status unavailable (curl 000), zero response bytes, and a successful connection in about 0.078 seconds. A minimal SPARQL control returned HTTP 200 in 0.135 seconds and the Wikidata entity API returned HTTP 200 in 0.251 seconds. This demonstrates a response deadline for the expensive query rather than failure to establish a connection; no upstream SPARQL timeout body was returned. The old query combined six date branches, Wikipedia article joins, optional classes/occupations/date precision, labels and global notability sorting. Smaller queries with global ordering or a forced disabled optimizer could still exceed the deadline, so both were removed. The default window is now ±5, explicitly authorized by the user's later message superseding the pasted ±10 requirement.
- Strategy: six date properties (P571 inception, P577 publication, P585 point in time, P580 start, P569 birth, P570 death) each have separate before/reference-year/after queries. Each retrieves only QID, date and sitelink count, using the date index/range-safe hint, the default optimizer and a bounded limit. No UNION, OPTIONAL, label service, subclass traversal, image joins or global ORDER BY remains. A raw page contains at most 240 rows (15 before + 10 same-year + 15 after per property), sampled through 18 small sequential requests. Balanced shortlists retain both sides before requesting English entity metadata for at most 60 distinct QIDs per window, in batches of at most 50. Laravel applies category, proximity and notability ranking, excluding minor appointments/skirmishes and low-notability battles. Wikipedia summaries and licensed 480px thumbnails/Commons attribution are requested only for the selected ten. These are bounded samples, not an exhaustive globally sorted Wikidata export.
- Date balance: reference-year candidates previously shared the after query and could crowd actual future events out of the shortlist. The three periods now have distinct query bounds and balanced shortlists. Selection reserves up to three suitable unseen events before and three after the reference year, while retaining up to three world events. Remaining places use the existing cultural ranking. An exhausted/sparse period falls back to other suitable candidates without inventing events; upstream partial failure can yield fewer than ten. The existing chronological Before/In/After admin presentation remains unchanged.
- Pagination/duplicates: bounded raw pages and shortlist windows provide further candidates inside ±5 rather than widening an increasingly expensive query. Saved/shown exclusions stay in Laravel/session state, with QID plus milestone identity independent of claim year; birth/death remain distinct and inception/publication/start claims for the same contextual event collapse. Only selected candidates leave the pool. Existing open searches preserve their exclusions and prior pool. Exhausted property/period pages are cached to avoid repeatedly querying empty branches. Successful raw pages, shortlists, entity metadata, normalized/redirected Wikipedia titles and Commons metadata are cached for six hours; failed partial results are not cached as complete searches.
- Reliability: new `WikimediaClient` identifies PianoLIT in its User-Agent, uses a three-second connection timeout, eight-second request limits and a 25-second discovery budget that reserves time for enrichment. It permits one brief retry for temporary non-timeout network/5xx failures, avoids repeating client/SPARQL timeouts, and honors numeric/date Retry-After via an endpoint cooldown without blocking or immediate retries. Logs include endpoint, actual HTTP status/error text, connection/response timeout distinctions, explicit upstream SPARQL timeout indicators, 403/429/5xx, query property and attempt. Technical errors remain server-side; the existing friendly admin error remains. During live checks, Wikidata returned HTTP 429 with Retry-After 120; subsequent queries were suppressed during that cooldown. After waiting beyond it, the remaining checks succeeded. Human-interactive entity requests omit maxlag; actual maxlag/rate-limit responses remain handled.
- Endpoints: `https://query.wikidata.org/sparql` for date discovery; `https://www.wikidata.org/w/api.php` (`wbgetentities`) for shortlisted entity metadata; `https://en.wikipedia.org/w/api.php` (`query` extracts/pageimages/info) for selected summaries/thumbnails; `https://commons.wikimedia.org/w/api.php` (`query` imageinfo/extmetadata) for thumbnail source/credit/license. No Wikipedia HTML scraping or original-image downloads.
- Affected files: new `app/Services/Timeline/WikimediaClient.php` and `tests/Review/WikimediaReliabilityTest.php`; changes to `WikimediaDiscovery.php`, admin `PieceTimelineController.php`, `config/wikimedia.php`, `tests/Review/PieceTimelineTest.php`, and this register. Some earlier changes were committed by the user during the task. No migration, asset rebuild, saved-event semantics, admin/public UI, public requests, iOS controller/resource or JSON contract changes. Unrelated user edits in admin piece table templates were preserved.
- Verification: focused isolated checks passed (23 tests / 351 assertions), including dominant same-year data, both-side selection, retained world quota, sparse fallback, pagination/cache reuse, stable duplicates, selected-only enrichment, optional enrichment failure, 403/429/5xx, numeric/date Retry-After, maxlag and connection/response/SPARQL timeout handling. All JavaScript checks and PHP syntax checks passed. The complete isolated review suite ran 171 tests / 6345 assertions with one unrelated collection-page wording failure at `WebAppCollectionsTest.php:46` expecting “The PianoLit path”; all other tests passed. Task-file diff checks pass; the whole working tree also reports an unrelated trailing blank line in the user's `pieces/table/info.blade.php` edit, left untouched.
- Live official endpoint checks through the isolated admin controller (memory SQLite, no normal-environment data writes): 1730 returned 10 (3 before / 3 same / 4 after), then 10 new, in 14.38 seconds combined; 1800 returned 10 (3 / 3 / 4), then 10 new, in 11.60 seconds; after respecting the rate-limit delay, 1850 returned 10 (3 / 3 / 4), then 10 new, in 19.80 seconds; 1900 returned 10 (3 / 2 / 5), then 10 new, in 15.41 seconds. All four produced zero duplicate milestone identities across both batches and all years remained within ±5. Earlier rate-limited/partial attempts were logged, not treated as successful complete searches. Upstream availability and coverage remain variable.
- Remaining work: deploy the client/service/controller/config together and refresh configured caches through the normal deployment process if applicable. Start a fresh search for the revised mix; an already visible list is not retroactively replaced. No production deployment, database/cache maintenance, migration or unrelated UI work was performed.

For a missing death date, the provisional default is birth plus 80 years, capped at the current year/date, preventing historical composers with unknown death dates from receiving centuries of unrelated context. Missing birth or reversed lifetime dates produce the existing empty state without inventing a piece year.

### 2026-10-02 — Faster, sortable admin pieces table with media filters (implemented locally)

- Priority: P2 demonstrated loading/sorting defects and requested filter controls. Removed only the leading audio/YouTube/Synthesia icon column. ID, Piece, Composer, Tags, Level, Rankings, Favorited and the trailing actions retain their existing order and contents; the existing Blade cell templates, badges/popups and action forms are reused unchanged. The Rankings cell currently displays the highlight date, so sorting that column uses the date without changing its label/content.
- The pieces-index endpoint now uses a scoped `Admin/PiecesTable` service. It selects only the two needed counts, eagerly loads tags/composer, hides model appends/relations before Yajra serializes them, and emits only the eight table fields. This avoids unused media/sibling queries and nested model payloads. The model's public/mobile appends and other datatable callers remain unchanged. Column clicks now determine the primary server ordering; the old fixed updated-at order previously overrode them. Sorting covers ID, piece/composer names, displayed tag count, difficulty including early/late variants, highlight date and favorite count, with a deterministic ID tie-breaker. Initial loading retains the updated-at descending order.
- Added three checked-by-default filters, as confirmed by the user: Pieces with videos (any tutorial), Pieces with sections (moments on any tutorial), and Pieces with Synthesia (type or legacy category). Checked requirements combine with AND; unchecking all shows every piece. Filters/search run before pagination and filtered counts, using existence queries so multiple videos/moments do not duplicate rows. Existing legacy URL filters remain supported. The new page-specific JavaScript resets pagination on filter changes, lets DataTables keep draws in order, ignores stale error notifications, releases the initial loading overlay on failure, and offers Retry.
- Validation bounds pagination/search/order shapes and fixes sortable SQL fields to server-owned column indices; client column names never become SQL. Global search covers piece naming/catalogue fields, IDs, composer names and tags. Action routes, admin guard, web CSRF forms and mobile payloads are preserved; no shared/personalized caching or migration.

PHP syntax, isolated production build/CSS checks, source/output/manifest consistency and git diff --check passed. Only the new versioned script was shipped from the isolated asset build.

### 2026-10-03 — Playlist reorder grip loses its drag (P2, complete)

Dragging near the viewport/player edge scrolls the list. SCSS supplies the lifted row and drop slot; tracked player JavaScript, app CSS and manifest were rebuilt. Regression coverage was added to `tests/js/playlist-player.js` and `tests/Review/PlaylistPlayerTest.php`.

The 12 focused isolated PHP tests passed (52 assertions), including persisted folder ordering and foreign-account rejection.
