### 2026-10-03 — Playlist audio loading on iOS (P2, implemented locally; device confirmation pending)

- **Evidence:** The user reported that a folder playable on desktop had only its last track working on iOS, with other durations missing. Inspection found background duration reads creating separate temporary Audio objects and manual track selections replacing the playback object. Physical iOS failure and the affected production files were not available for reproduction. WebKit recommends retaining a media element when switching sources because autoplay permission is per element (`https://webkit.org/blog/7734/auto-play-policy-changes-for-macos/`); its issue 247261 also records problems with pages creating many audio elements. These support reducing element churn, but do not establish the exact cause of the user's failure.
- **Affected files / change:** The shared player source/public JavaScript and manifest entry, `tests/js/playlist-player.js`, and this register. Every track selection now reuses one playback element. Nearby duration reads use one retained loader sequentially; a playback request cancels its source, listeners, timer, queue and observer before starting audio. No further background loads run after playback is requested. Both loading and playback handle `durationchange`, allowing a duration that was unknown at `loadedmetadata` to appear later. Cancelled callbacks cannot overwrite a played track's duration, and failed/timed-out metadata reads advance the queue.
- **Compatibility:** Folder/collection actions still invoke play synchronously from the user's tap, and automatic next, loop, speed, preview limits, super-user/free-pick access and hidden-on-load behavior remain intact. Unread durations may remain blank until their track is played once background probing is stopped. Media URLs/formats, server delivery, routes and mobile JSON contracts are unchanged.
- **Verification:** The new manual-selection reuse assertion failed against the previous script (two playback objects instead of one), then passed after implementation. All JavaScript regressions pass, including delayed finite durations, loader cancellation/timeouts, stale callbacks, automatic advancement, replay and preview boundaries. Isolated production build/CSS checks, source/output comparison and `git diff --check` pass. Isolated folder/guest-collection Blade checks pass (2 tests / 20 assertions). Browser switching through all seven available fixture tracks showed active playback and valid 20-second durations without errors; the 390px preview retained the shared styling. These fixtures share a synthetic WAV recording and were tested in the desktop browser, not iOS.
- **Follow-up:** The user confirmed the failure persisted. The production-recording MIME investigation below reproduces the unsupported source in Safari and resolves that demonstrated defect; the retained-element and cancellation changes remain in place. Screenshot: `ios-loading-fix.jpg` under the local playlist-player visualization folder.

### 2026-10-03 — Use device volume for mobile playlists (P3, complete)

- **Evidence:** The user requested synchronized hardware volume or removal of the playlist volume control. Apple documents that JavaScript cannot read/set iOS device volume; the browser's media-volume property is independent of hardware volume on platforms that allow it (`https://developer.apple.com/library/archive/documentation/AudioVideo/Conceptual/Using_HTML5_Audio_Video/Device-SpecificConsiderations/Device-SpecificConsiderations.html`). Implemented the requested fallback instead of displaying a misleading second control.
- **Affected files / behavior:** Shared playlist JavaScript/SCSS, tracked script/app CSS and manifest entries, the existing JavaScript regression, and this register. At widths through 991px or with a coarse primary pointer, volume and mute controls are hidden and playback uses media volume 1 with no independent mute. Media-query changes update the active element and controls; desktop volume/mute settings are retained for returning to desktop. Phone transport controls now span the freed row and are centered. Wide touch layouts omit the unused volume column.
- **Compatibility:** Folder and collection players share the fallback. Desktop volume controls remain available; preview enforcement, media loading changes, speed, navigation, media URLs and mobile API contracts are unchanged. The earlier iOS playback report still awaits physical-device confirmation.
- **Verification:** JavaScript regressions pass, including full mobile media volume, absence of independent mute, hidden-control guards, desktop controls and setting restoration. Isolated production build/CSS checks, source/output comparison and `git diff --check` pass. Rendered SQLite fixtures pass the focused folder/guest-collection checks (2 tests / 20 assertions). Browser previews confirmed hidden volume/mute at 390px and 800px, no phone horizontal overflow, centered transport at x=195 on a 390px viewport, and restored controls at desktop width. Screenshot: `device-volume-mobile.jpg` under the local playlist-player visualization folder. Hardware volume itself was not read or changed; no remaining implementation work for this fallback.


### 2026-10-03 — Safari rejects legacy playlist MPGA sources (P2, complete locally)

- **Evidence:** The user's desktop/iPhone screenshots identify folder 155, Book 3, and the failing `2. Andante Op.36 No.1` (piece 270). Read-only inspection of the ten piece audio endpoints found nine `.mpga` recordings and one `.mp4` recording (piece 672), matching the nine failures and working last track. The failing URL returns `application/octet-stream`; its byte-range request succeeds with HTTP 206 and a correct Content-Range. ffprobe confirms a valid 44.1 kHz stereo MP3, duration 93.858 seconds. The working file is H.264/AAC MP4, served as `video/mp4` with valid byte ranges. In Mac Safari, the exact production MPGA URL loaded with `audio.src` fails with MEDIA_ERR_SRC_NOT_SUPPORTED (4) and NaN duration. The same URL in a `<source type="audio/mpeg">` loads its 1:33 duration and plays. This is a reproduced format-identification defect, superseding the earlier unconfirmed resource-churn explanation. WebKit describes source type as a parser hint when server/URL identification is insufficient: [issue 198015, comment 3](https://bugs.webkit.org/show_bug.cgi?id=198015#c3).
- **Affected files / fix:** `resources/js/views/playlist-player.js`, its tracked public copy and manifest entry, `tests/js/playlist-player.js`, and this register. Playback and metadata now share a retained source child with an explicit type for MPGA/MP3 (`audio/mpeg`), MP4 (`video/mp4`) and M4A (`audio/mp4`). Selecting a different format updates the hint; unrecognized extensions remove the old hint and retain browser detection. No direct audio src overrides the child. Cancellation removes the child's source before load, releasing background media.
- **Compatibility:** Original public storage URLs, file contents, mobile JSON, routes and access rules are unchanged. Playback still starts synchronously from the tap, reuses its authorized element, preserves preview boundaries/loop/speed and keeps the dock hidden until requested. Volume remains device-controlled on mobile. No server configuration change, conversion, production data write or deployment was performed.
- **Verification:** The new typed-source regression fails against unchanged HEAD and passes with this fix. All JavaScript regressions pass, including mixed-format source replacement, unknown-type clearing, metadata typing/cancellation, stale events, preview limits and existing controls. The focused isolated playlist PHP suite passes (7 tests / 44 assertions); isolated production build and CSS checks pass, source/output match and the script's version hash changed. Mac Safari using rendered isolated Blade markup with the actual ten production recording URLs displayed all ten durations before playback; Play all and Next played every track, with advancing elapsed positions and matching durations. Switching from the final MP4 back to the first MPGA also played. The exact original/fixed source comparison and repaired playlist screenshots are saved under the local `playlist-audio-mime` visualization directory. `git diff --check` passes.
- **Remaining verification:** Deploy the script and manifest together and reload folder 155 on the physical iPhone. Physical iOS hardware was unavailable, so Mac Safari reproduction and production-file playback are verified, while the final device check remains pending. An eventual static-server MIME mapping for `.mpga` would also correct direct-file identification; this playlist fix does not require changing delivery or existing URLs.

### 2026-10-06 — Admin composer bio regeneration with OpenAI (P2, complete locally; server key required)

- **Request / change:** Composer edit pages now show Regenerate bio to admins with the existing update permission. A session-authenticated, CSRF-protected POST sends the saved composer name and current biography text to OpenAI's Responses API. The prompt requires simple English, everyday words, short sentences, no jargon or complex language, and only facts in the source. Structured output and server validation enforce one to three nonempty paragraphs, each at most 60 words and 600 characters. Incomplete, refused, malformed, oversized and HTML responses are rejected.

Loading disables repeated generation and saving; failures retain the source and restore controls. Bio edits made during a request invalidate its result, including editing and restoring the same value. Empty/oversized source validation, safe upstream errors, bounded connection/request timeouts and a 10-per-minute admin limit keep requests controlled.

The server uses `OPENAI_API_KEY` with configurable `OPENAI_MODEL` (default `gpt-4o-mini`); the existing inbound `CHATGPT_TOKEN` remains separate. No dependencies or migrations were added. Existing composer saving, admin host/guard/policy rules, mobile JSON contracts, membership and media delivery are unchanged.
- **Verification:** Composer/admin-subdomain checks pass (46 tests / 3683 assertions), including actual CSRF middleware enforcement, guest/web-user denial, editor ownership, manager access, current unsaved source, no database write during generation, server-selected model, output limits/refusal/shape validation, safe failures and throttling. Browser checks with the actual rendered Blade fixture and a mocked API response confirm button placement, loading and restored controls, draft insertion and preservation of edits during a request.
- **Full-suite findings:** The full isolated PHP suite ran 331 tests / 11962 assertions and reported 3 errors and 7 failures in `TimelineEventsTest` and `WebTimelineLibraryTest`. The exact same failing tests reproduce against an isolated copy of unchanged HEAD (23 tests / 176 assertions), including SQLite's unsupported `YEAR()` query and existing timeline selection/content/query-count expectations. These predate this feature and remain separate work.

### 2026-10-06 — Bulk composer biography rewrite command (P2, complete locally)

- **Request / change:** Added `php artisan composers:regenerate-bios` to rewrite and immediately save every composer's existing biography using the same `ComposerBiography` service and configured model as the admin button. The command makes one sequential API request per eligible composer, processes database batches of 50, skips empty bios, rejects sources beyond the editor's 20000-character limit, displays progress and returns a failure exit code for unfinished rewrites. Repeatable `--composer=ID` options and a printed targeted retry command avoid reprocessing successes.

Failed API calls preserve source text and processing continues. Backup failure stops processing before saving that draft; the retry list includes remaining composers. Each save uses a short transaction and row lock after generation, with strict PHP comparisons of name/source to preserve concurrent edits, including case-only changes. Transactions do not span API calls. Backup entries may describe a failed/conflicted save and are not a success ledger.
- **Affected files / compatibility:** New `app/Console/Commands/RegenerateComposerBiographies.php`, isolated command regressions, `docs/OPENAI_ADMIN.md` and this register. Laravel's existing command-directory discovery registers the command. Constructor has no external work. Only biography and its normal update timestamp are saved through Eloquent; other fields and API contracts remain unchanged. No dependencies, migrations, queue worker, scheduler, assets, `.env` edits or normal-environment database writes were needed.
- **Verification:** Isolated command and existing composer-generation checks pass (54 tests / 242 assertions). Coverage includes all-composer processing across the 50-row boundary, one request each, configured model/current source, original backups/private visibility, selective retries and invalid IDs, empty/oversized sources, safe partial API failures, concurrent case-only bio edits, rollback after an update failure, backup failures and missing configuration. PHP lint and `git diff --check` pass.
- **Remaining work / limits:** Deploy the command with the existing generation service, then the user can run it against the intended site database. ### 2026-10-06 — Configurable generation budget and specific incomplete-bio errors (P2, complete locally)

- **Evidence / diagnosis:** The bulk-run screenshot shows composer #18 rejected as incomplete while adjacent composers saved. The existing message proves the API returned `status: incomplete` but hides its reason; the exact response and selected production model were not supplied. Official OpenAI documentation confirms the output budget includes internal reasoning tokens, and reaching that budget can produce an incomplete response before visible text is finished. The hardcoded 1600-token budget is a plausible cause, not a confirmed diagnosis of this request. A content-filter interruption can also produce an incomplete response.
- **Change / affected files:** `config/services.php` now exposes `OPENAI_MAX_OUTPUT_TOKENS` (default 4096; project range 256–32768). `ComposerBiography.php` validates the server value and sends it to the API, and distinguishes token-limit and content-filter reasons using fixed safe messages. Unknown reasons retain the generic incomplete message. Updated service/command regressions, `docs/OPENAI_ADMIN.md` and this register. Browser-submitted token budgets cannot override server settings.
- **Compatibility:** The 3–4 paragraph count, 100-word/1000-character caps, source-only rewriting and configured model remain unchanged. Both admin button and bulk command use the same service. No automatic retry, additional API call per generation, timeout changes, assets, dependencies, environment edits, mobile JSON changes or normal-environment writes were introduced. Failed bulk generations still preserve the original and continue.
- **Verification:** Isolated service/command checks pass (62 tests / 273 assertions), including configured/default token budgets, invalid configuration before HTTP, safe known/unknown incomplete reasons, unchanged paragraph caps and incomplete bulk generation preserving the original while the next composer succeeds. PHP lint and `git diff --check` pass. Verification used mocked API responses and disposable database/storage.
- **Remaining work / limits:** Deploy the service/config changes and refresh cached configuration through normal deployment. Retry unfinished IDs using the bulk command's targeted options. The screenshot's exact API response was not recovered and no live paid request or production bulk rewrite was executed. Increased token headroom reduces token-budget risk but does not guarantee every model/source will finish within the existing request timeout.

A reading response after Back/restart cannot fetch an obsolete PDF, and a late player load cannot enhance a disposed result. Native result playback remains available if Plyr fails. Added focused PHP/JS regressions and rebuilt `public/js/views/match-tour.js` with its Mix manifest entry.
- **Compatibility:** Existing route names, page styling, quiz engine, result modal, answer validation, preview limits, membership rules, mobile/iOS JSON contracts and public media URLs remain unchanged. No shared catalog cache, migration, dependency update, environment edit or normal-environment database write was introduced.

Headless Chrome using the real rendered Blade and shipped assets verified immediate first-question rendering without PDF/Plyr requests, desktop/390px layout, delayed PDF loading, Back during loading, library reuse, all questionnaire steps, native result modal, preview cutoff and stale player cleanup. Desktop/narrow screenshots were inspected; fixture composer artwork was unavailable. Browser media libraries/results were mocked to control delays and avoid real media requests.

### 2026-10-07 — Prepare opt-in YouTube campaign attribution (implemented locally; disabled)

- **Scope / flow:** Added exact-allowlist acknowledgement of the Respighi video link on piece 174, explicit opt-in/withdrawal, encrypted host-only 30-day cookies and web-guard account binding through signup/login. Direct navigation and refreshes do not overwrite the first eligible touch; logout clears cookies. No arbitrary URLs, query values, contact/card data or guest browsing history are added. Signed-in withdrawal removes owned campaign links, and account deletion cascades them.
- **Billing / attribution:** Web purchase records the exact returned Stripe subscription and counts a trial only for provider-confirmed `trialing`. A separate minimal signed positive-invoice receipt ledger reconciles invoices arriving before local linkage. Database uniqueness deduplicates invoice/charge identities; a transactional charge marker prevents additional legacy `payments` inserts on retries and rolls back on handler failure. Reports match subscription AND customer, count only the earliest positive successful invoice per subscription and exclude renewals/zero/failed/manual/shop payments. Historical duplicate payments are untouched. Apple/mobile contracts, existing membership logic, billing API payloads, GTM and Meta settings are unchanged.
- **Surface / affected files:** New attribution configuration, migration, services, consent controller/route/component, web middleware, standalone JavaScript and manifest entry. Focused edits to web purchase/Stripe factory and webhooks; read-only `attribution:report --since=YYYY-MM-DD` CLI and daily `attribution:prune`. No admin report UI or GA4 exporter. Details and release checklist: `docs/CAMPAIGN_ATTRIBUTION.md`.
- **Verification:** Affected isolated PHP suites pass (44 tests / 1088 assertions), covering guest consent/navigation, real signup/login, cross-account ownership, mocked web checkout, actual signed invoice dispatch, trial versus immediate payment, invoice/charge retries, invoice-before-binding/out-of-order delivery, withdrawal, expiry, retention and database uniqueness. Project JavaScript regressions, isolated production asset build (including CSS checks), PHP syntax and diff checks pass. The enabled consent component was visually checked using real Blade output and built CSS at desktop, 390px and 320px; no production application was started. The full review run has 3 timeline errors and 7 timeline failures reproduced on committed HEAD, plus one timestamp-only collection comparison failure; that collection test passes on committed HEAD and is rechecked separately. Those unrelated tests were not edited. SQLite uniqueness checks do not exercise simultaneous MySQL transactions or real Stripe delivery.
- **Activation / remaining work:** `CAMPAIGN_ATTRIBUTION_ENABLED` defaults false, so the feature is dormant until approved deployment. Apply `2026_10_07_030000_create_campaign_attribution_tables.php`, review opt-in wording/30-day attribution/90-day retention and separate financial-receipt handling, verify both US/Swiss signed Stripe endpoints/API payloads and delivery of `invoice.payment_succeeded`, confirm the scheduler, then perform a disposable sandbox integration test before enabling. This consent control covers first-party campaign measurement, not all pre-existing GTM/Meta tracking; no legal-compliance claim. No production migrations, secrets/settings, live purchases, deployment, push or YouTube edit performed.

- **Request / evidence:** The final explanation said no close match was found and offered a free pick. The user requested a positive best-match explanation and an actual overlap, even through one mood or difficulty answer.

It reuses the historical/media scopes and exclusions, ranks by normalized difficulty, requested tags and listening-character overlap, breaks ties by difficulty proximity, and randomizes only equal best fits. Early/late difficulty labels normalize to their existing base range. If no exact trait overlaps, it selects the nearest tagged difficulty; candidates without any supporting tag/known difficulty do not receive an invented match. `QuizFactory.php` opts this web branch into the existing complete-level guard for strict similarity, allowing candidates without levels to be evaluated safely by the broader mood pass. Internal context records actual level overlap/nearest-level evidence. `MatchTour.php` uses “Here is the best match we could find, with a video and score ready for you.” and adds only supported reasons; it no longer reports that no match was found or describes an arbitrary free pick.
- **Compatibility:** The perfect match remains a historical free pick with video and an in-app score; additional recommendations retain their existing broader pool. Existing keyword parsing, strict ranking, route validation, display elements and media/player/access rules are reused. Default public/mobile search and payloads remain unchanged; broader matching is enabled only by the web tour’s existing opt-in. Prior tour animation/card/style source and built assets were preserved. Added focused coverage for split-level-only, selected-mood-only with actual result HTML/JSON, listening-character-only, mood-only without a level tag, stronger overlap beyond five candidates, nearest difficulty and explanation/state reset, history/media/exclusions, and unsupported untagged candidates. Partial explanation reuses loaded tags without additional queries. Stabilized the older randomized fallback fixture so it consistently exercises unmatched difficulty. PHP syntax and `git diff --check` pass.

### 2026-10-08 — Start tour audio at random middle excerpts (P3, complete locally)

- **Implementation / affected files:** `resources/js/views/match-tour.js` reuses `AnimatedCount` with an optional change callback so the existing SVG arc follows every displayed count, including animated frames, Back, cancellation and error recovery. The colored arc is remaining count / initial total; at one piece the remaining cap disappears. Section navigation retains its dots, labels and themes independently. `resources/sass/components/_match-tour.scss` retains round caps, theme colors and the light border track, with a small elastic stretch while counting. Stroke geometry follows the number immediately, avoiding a separate easing animation that could lag or complete too early. Reduced-motion mode skips the stretch. Added focused linked-counter regressions and updated shipped app CSS/tour JavaScript and their manifest hashes.
- **Compatibility:** Existing matching, estimates, result-request handling, reveal duration, full video/random audio, media access and mobile contracts are unchanged. No extra result delay, duplicate counter/player, new dependency, migration or deployment.
- **Verification:** Full JavaScript regressions and isolated production build/app-admin CSS-variable checks pass. Actual route/Blade fixtures in Chrome at 1440×900 and 390×844 verify full initial rings, proportional half-count rings, incomplete mood/waiting arcs and frame-by-frame synchronization through delayed and fast result responses. The last cap disappears only when the displayed count is one, before the counter hides for the result. Failure and Back restore count/arc together; stale results cannot replace the recovered screen; reduced-motion updates immediately. Desktop/phone ring screenshots inspected; built/shipped equality, final manifest hashes and `git diff --check` pass.
- **Remaining work / limits:** Deploy the ring Blade markup from the preceding change with app CSS, tour JavaScript and manifest; no deployment performed. Browser checks use isolated repertoire/result fixtures. Physical Safari/Firefox devices were not exercised.

### 2026-10-08 — Review and harden the web Find your match flow (P2, complete locally)

- **Scope / evidence:** Reviewed the web route/controller, encrypted draw and answer validation, catalog/example selection, Blade modal/progress/result views, shared card rendering, audio/video previews, score loading, animation/navigation lifecycle, ranking, explanations and additional recommendations. The shared engine randomly selected among the top five even when some had weaker requested-mood scores, and compared level tag IDs to infer difficulty. It repeated keyword/choice/candidate queries. Additional recommendations hydrated every shared-mood piece before taking four. The stage entrance animation's `both` fill mode overrode the later exit transition. PDF timeouts covered document loading but not page retrieval/rendering; abandoned players retained media resources, and a page-cache restore could leave a cancelled transition/result permanently busy.
- **Implementation / affected files:** Added `app/Services/WebApp/MatchQuiz.php`, used only by `WebApp/MatchTourController`. It batches keyword/choice/candidate loading, removes unused counts/composer-country loads, matches the verified playing/reading range by level names (including split early/late names), and randomizes only among all equally strongest requested-tag matches. Listening choices contribute musical taste independently of their difficulty. Existing eligibility, exclusions, nearest/partial fallback and explanation logic remain inherited. `MatchTour::recommendations()` applies shared mood, exact extended difficulty, media eligibility and self-exclusion before a four-row SQL limit; cards reuse loaded relations. Tour JavaScript bounds the entire score operation, cancels abandoned score work, ignores late render completion, handles worker cleanup rejections, releases media on close/restart, restores cancelled work after a page-cache return, resets result-shell styling on restart, and avoids rewriting an unchanged counter value. The SCSS entrance animation uses backwards fill so exit transitions work. Updated focused PHP/JavaScript regressions, shipped tour JavaScript/app CSS and final-file manifest hashes.
- **Compatibility:** No edits to `routes/api`, API controllers, `App/Api`, shared `FindYourMatch` engine, models, mobile serialization or media/PDF URLs. Public funnel/mobile behavior remains unchanged. Preserved the fullscreen design, two taste pairs, encrypted version-one/two/three in-flight draws, guest access, server-derived identity/ability, historical-free-pick main-result eligibility, exact-level two-to-four optional recommendations, and existing preview durations. No migrations, dependency changes or deployment.
- **Performance evidence:** The same isolated 48-piece fixture and three listening choices used 29 queries through the original engine versus 6 through the web adapter. A regression asserts six matching queries and fewer than the original engine; this is a fixture measurement, not a production latency claim. Additional recommendations issue three queries, hydrate at most four pieces, omit unused count subqueries, and render their cards with zero additional queries. Existing 612-piece catalog tests retain bounded opening-query counts.
- **Verification:** Focused MatchTour tests pass (54 tests / 823 assertions), including strongest/tied ranking, playing range independent of listening difficulty/tag IDs, SQL limits, loaded-card reuse, exact sublevels, eligibility/exclusions, fallback, guest/result routes, invalid/expired/cross-round draws and backward-compatible older draws. Full JavaScript regressions, isolated production build and app/admin CSS-variable checks pass. Actual route/Blade fixtures in Chrome at 1440, 390 and 320 pixels exercise the full questionnaire with real PDF.js rendering, result/options/Back, restart, playback, modal close/reopen, ordinary-motion exit/Back interruption, failed-result retry, simulated persisted pagehide/pageshow and stale-response rejection. Screenshots inspected; no page JavaScript errors. PHP/JavaScript syntax, shipped/source equality and `git diff --check` pass. The final full isolated run completed 482 tests / 13,581 assertions. Its unrelated timeline/page-expectation failures reproduce with the original web controller/service loaded from a temporary baseline: three errors and nine failures (SQLite `YEAR`, timeline selection/query-count expectations, composer-link and folder-heading expectations).

### 2026-10-09 — Smooth Explore slides and add a legacy-browser fallback (P2, implemented locally; iPad confirmation pending)

- **Request / evidence:** Slides felt abrupt and did not run on the user's iPad despite the full-width directory layout. The prior code depended entirely on pageswap/pagereveal and a non-null native ViewTransition. The iPad browser/OS version remains unknown; WebKit documents native cross-document support starting in Safari/iPadOS 18.2.
- **Implementation / affected files:** `_explore.scss` now uses 360ms easing with a gentle start/finish, 3D transforms and compositor hints. `explore-transitions.js` records direction from ordinary link clicks as well as pageswap. Browsers without pagereveal use DOMContentLoaded and persisted pageshow; a null native transition also uses the fallback. The CSS fallback animates arriving main content and its fixed top path, leaving the bottom menu outside the transformed element; return uses a subtle slide/fade. Native browsers retain the existing full-page overlay effect. Cleanup removes temporary styles after the animation. Rebuilt app CSS and the script in an isolated production build and updated final-byte manifest hashes.
- **Compatibility:** Keeps ordinary navigation, route/query contracts, mobile APIs, breakpoint, current 74px offset, header styling, desktop layout and reduced-motion behavior. The older-browser fallback is an arrival animation rather than a retained snapshot of the prior page. No user-agent sniffing, history rewriting, dependency changes or HTML-fetch routing.
- **Verification:** Full JS regressions pass, including no native events, forward/back fallback, timer cleanup, null native transition, reduced motion, direct visits, storage failure and native history behavior. Production build and app/admin CSS-variable checks pass. Live 834px portrait checks observe a native forward transition with 0.36s duration and the new easing, no duplicate fallback, and a backward transition restoring the directory menu. Diff checks pass.
- **Remaining work / limits:** Not deployed. Native animation was verified in a tablet-sized desktop browser, and missing APIs were simulated in regression tests. The reported physical iPad failure has not been reproduced or confirmed resolved on that device; browser/OS details are still needed if it persists. No frame-rate/performance benchmark was performed.

### 2026-10-09 — Add country continents and a one-time Artisan backfill (P3, complete locally)

- **Request / evidence:** Countries previously contained only name, nationality, flag code and timestamps; add a nullable continent string and a manually run Artisan command to populate every existing country.
- **Implementation / affected files:** Added `2026_10_09_180000_add_continent_to_countries_table.php`, `FillCountryContinents.php` (`countries:fill-continents`, optional `--dry-run`), and a 252-entry GeoNames snapshot in `resources/data/country-continents.json`. The command matches normalized ISO flag codes before exact normalized names/explicit aliases, reports every unmatched row before writing, and updates only continent in one transaction. Correct rows are skipped on repeat runs. `docs/COUNTRY_CONTINENTS.md` records execution instructions, source attribution/license and classification rules.
- **Compatibility:** No network/API credentials or dependency changes required at execution. Existing names, flags, nationalities, timestamps and relationships are preserved. `Country.php` hides continent from default array/JSON serialization to preserve mobile contracts while exposing the PHP attribute. GeoNames supplies one continent per country (Russia: Europe; Turkey: Asia). Unknown/historical names outside the dataset require an explicit correction/mapping, never fuzzy guessing. No frontend changes or build required.
- **Verification:** Focused isolated command suite passes (8 tests / 579 assertions), covering all 252 mappings, seven labels, aliases, null/empty/replaced values, dry runs, repeated runs, unmatched aborts, transaction rollback after a write failure, missing schema, migration reversal and unchanged country JSON. PHP syntax and diff checks pass. The full isolated suite ran 510 tests / 14,550 assertions and reported 3 errors (SQLite YEAR in timeline admin views) and 10 failures across TimelineEvents, WebTimelineLibrary, WebAppGuestAccess and the Explore query budget; these areas were not changed or repaired in this task, and no baseline comparison was run.
- **Remaining work / limits:** Migration and backfill have not run on the application database. Deploy, migrate, inspect the dry run and execute the command. Actual catalogue coverage and MySQL locking/rollback remain unverified; tests use isolated in-memory SQLite. Migration rollback uses native DROP COLUMN, supported by MySQL and SQLite >= 3.35, avoiding an optional Doctrine dependency in this Laravel version.

### 2026-10-09 — Prevent iPad Explore auto-zoom during page slides (P2, implemented locally; physical-device confirmation pending)

The guard is outside the mobile media query so a transient width expansion cannot disable it. Removed the narrower, temporary fallback-only overflow rule. Added an isolated regression verifying device-width plus shrink-to-fit protection for both directory and guide, while Highlights retains its previous viewport. Rebuilt app CSS in an isolated production build and updated the final-byte manifest hash.
- **Compatibility:** Existing zoom settings, breakpoints, animation timing, current header styling/offset, menus, navigation, other pages, media rules and mobile API contracts remain unchanged.
- **Verification:** Explore suite passes (19 tests / 468 assertions), production build and app/admin CSS-variable checks pass. At 820px in the live browser, document client/scroll widths both remain 820px before, during and after the forward slide and during the back slide; the completed guide retains its full-width panel and hidden directory. Computed document overflow is clip and native overlay overflow is hidden. Diff checks pass.
- **Remaining work / limits:** Not deployed. This addresses the failure mechanism seen in the recording, but confirmation on the physical iPad is still needed; desktop viewport emulation cannot reproduce all iPad Safari auto-zoom behavior. Reference: https://bugs.webkit.org/show_bug.cgi?id=271819.

### 2026-10-09 — Pinch to zoom the score viewport (P3, complete locally; physical-device verification pending)

- **Request / evidence:** A Mac trackpad or iPhone/iPad pinch within the score should operate the score zoom controls rather than magnify the whole page.
- **Implementation / affected files:** `resources/js/views/score-editor.js` binds non-passive Ctrl-wheel, two-touch and Safari gesture handlers to `.score-scroll`. Small movements accumulate into the existing 25% zoom steps, bounded at 50%–250%; the existing renderer queues the latest zoom during busy PDF rendering. The viewport/read-mode sheet allow pan-x/pan-y without native pinch zoom. Touches must start inside the viewport; pinch cancels unfinished strokes/text drags, releases pointer capture, and suppresses drawing until both fingers lift. Duplicate Safari/wheel events during a touch gesture are ignored. Updated focused JS regressions, rebuilt the public script and updated its manifest hash; preserved the preceding redo changes.
- **Compatibility:** Ordinary scrolling and page zoom outside the score remain available. No global viewport restriction, annotation schema/ownership, access, PDF delivery, route or mobile API changes. This affects the mobile web score viewer only.
- **References:** Browser event handling follows [MDN wheel events](https://developer.mozilla.org/en-US/docs/Web/API/Element/wheel_event), [touch-action](https://developer.mozilla.org/en-US/docs/Web/CSS/Reference/Properties/touch-action), [multitouch events](https://developer.mozilla.org/en-US/docs/Web/API/Touch_events/Multi-touch_interaction) and the [WebKit gesture fallback](https://developer.mozilla.org/en-US/docs/Web/API/Element/gesturestart_event).
- **Verification:** Focused score tests and the full JS suite pass (existing unrelated alert warnings remain). Tests cover wheel delta units, both directions, accumulation, limits, absent/busy PDFs, queued rendering, inside/outside touches, one-finger scrolling, gesture cancellation, pointer capture and duplicate-event suppression. Browser fixture dispatches synthetic wheel/touch/Safari events through the actual bound listeners and renderer, confirming 75%→100%→75% changes, resized canvas/sheet, prevented defaults inside and unprevented events outside. Touch events also update zoom at a 390px viewport; the body keeps touch-action auto. Isolated production build, app/admin CSS-variable checks and diff checks pass.
- **Remaining work / limits:** Not deployed. Browser integration used synthetic gesture events and a fake PDF/save adapter; physical Mac trackpad, Safari/iPhone/iPad native pinch prevention and actual PDFs still require device verification. These checks do not prove OS/browser gesture dispatch or prevention on physical hardware.

### 2026-10-10 — Intermittent production web-app latency (P1, investigation open)

- **Evidence:** User reports slow navigation across `my.pianolit.com`, including signed-out Incognito. Read-only public GET measurements returned HTTP 200: Explore first-byte times 0.716 s, 25.888 s, and 19.625 s; Discover 15.674 s. Main CSS, app JavaScript and one period artwork image completed in 0.202 s, 0.285 s and 0.366 s respectively. A subsequent `/login` request completed in 0.156 s (TLS completed at 0.082 s). These samples demonstrate intermittent delay before catalogue HTML; they do not identify the production bottleneck. The visible PianoLIT logo is the normal page header, not a dedicated loader in the current layout.
- **Affected code / hypotheses:** `app/Services/WebApp/ExploreCatalogue.php::moodImage()` fetches tags separately for each uncovered candidate while searching for unused artwork. An ephemeral isolated HTTP test with 12 overlapping moods and 200 pieces without covers or period tags produced 2,222 SQL queries, versus 21 with distinct covers (50-piece comparison: 572 versus 21). This deliberately adverse fixture demonstrates unbounded query growth, not production query counts. `app/Http/Middleware/UpdateLocation.php` also performs a synchronous external lookup on every signed-in request, with three sequential HTTP providers permitting two seconds each; guests skip that lookup. Discover additionally uses Redis/cache through `app/Api/Api.php`. Shared database contention or PHP worker queues remain hypotheses requiring server evidence.
- **Compatibility:** Investigation only; application code, production configuration, data, search credentials, mobile APIs and media delivery remain unchanged. Any subsequent optimization must preserve artwork behavior, account isolation and mobile contracts.
- **Verification / remaining work:** Public request timing, live browser inspection, source review and an in-memory SQLite probe only. No production shell, slow-query log, PHP-FPM queue/slowlog, database lock/connection metrics, or Redis timings were available. Inspect those during a slow request to attribute the site-wide delay, then bound artwork query growth and remove repeated location lookups from the web request path with focused regressions. No fix or production performance guarantee is claimed.

### 2026-10-10 — Reduce catalogue database work and defer optional web location lookup (P1, implemented locally; rollout pending)

- **Evidence:** Follow-up to the latency investigation above. Repository migrations lack indexes for several foreign-key lookups used by global Piece/Composer relation counts and web media badges. Isolated query plans demonstrate repeated scans of `piece_views`, `favorites`, `tutorials`, `performances`, `pieces` by composer, and `piece_tag` by tag. Existing Explore artwork selection also repeats tag reads after candidate artwork is exhausted. A final pre-deployment production Discover sample returned its first byte in 0.477 s and completed in 0.555 s, confirming variability; no local change had reached production.
- **Implementation / affected files:** `2026_10_10_140000_add_catalogue_relation_indexes.php` adds six reversible non-unique indexes: `piece_id` on views/favorites/tutorials/performances, `pieces.composer_id`, and `piece_tag(tag_id, piece_id)`. `ExploreCatalogue` batches candidate IDs/cover paths and relevant mood/period metadata once, then selects random distinct images in memory. `UpdateLocation` skips accounts with stored locations, scopes retry timestamps to the authenticated account's browser session, and performs missing web lookups in terminable middleware after the response is sent. Failed optional lookups retry at most hourly per browser (`config/webapp.php`); writes use the account's location relation. Tests added in `CatalogueRelationIndexesTest`, `ExploreArtworkQueriesTest`, and `WebLocationLatencyTest`.

### 2026-10-10 — Interactive composer globe (P3, complete locally)

- **Request / evidence:** Populate the existing fullscreen composer modal with a rotatable, zoomable globe; select continents at a distance and countries when closer, showing each place's composer and piece totals.
- **Implementation / affected files:** `ComposerGlobe` aggregates eligible catalogue composers and their pieces by recorded country in one SQL read, using the same `Composer::atLeast(1)` eligibility as the directory. The new public web-only `webapp.composers.globe` GET route precedes the route-bound composer path. The index validates a seven-value continent filter for continent browse links; country links retain the existing country filter. `webapp/composers/globe.blade.php`, `resources/js/views/composer-globe.js` and scoped `_composers.scss` provide continent geometry/labels, country borders at closer zoom, hover tooltips, gold selections, live counts, browse links, a keyboard-accessible place picker, reset/zoom controls and responsive details. Camera altitude uses hysteresis for stable continent/country transitions. Country borders are separate paths excluded from picking. Render failures leave the place picker available; failed requests can retry. Loading is deferred until the modal opens, close-during-load never starts a hidden renderer, and hidden modal/page rendering pauses.
- **Assets / provenance:** Added the unchanged local UMD `globe.gl@2.46.2` distribution with its MIT license, plus Natural Earth v5.1.2 public-domain 1:110m world and 1:50m country geometry. The map retains only geometry, country code/name/continent and label coordinates, rounds coordinates to three decimals, and combines geometries sharing an ISO code (240 selectable countries). Distant continents use the 177-feature coarse layer; the detailed layer omits island polygons below 0.05 square degrees except each country's largest polygon, reducing its polygon count from 1,620 to 769 while retaining all countries. Country names/continent conventions use the existing crosswalk. Source/provenance notes are in `resources/data/composer-globe.README.md`. `webpack.mix.js` copies and versions the map, vendor library and controller. Rebuilt app CSS, controller/map/vendor outputs and corresponding manifest hashes; generated the required zoom-in icon. No CDN request is needed at runtime and the legacy dependency toolchain remains unchanged.
- **Compatibility:** Whole-catalogue counts are independent of route-scoped directory filters and temporary browser filters. Countries without repertoire show zero; composers without a recorded continent remain in world totals, with an explanatory note. Recorded countries absent from map geometry remain selectable in the picker. No account identity comes from request parameters, and no personal models or piece media are serialized. Existing composer routes/search/facets, country mobile serialization, API contracts, membership access and media delivery remain intact. No migration, deployment, database write, billing or queue command was run.
- **Verification:** Focused isolated composer/globe/geography PHP checks pass (15 tests / 681 assertions, including 7 composer/globe tests / 102 assertions), retaining the existing three-query directory check and measuring one query for globe aggregation. Tests cover guest access, catalogue eligibility, multiple composers/pieces per country, missing geography, empty totals, matching browse links and invalid continent shapes. Focused globe JS passes with strict unhandled-rejection handling; the full JS runner completes with its existing unrelated mock-alert promise warnings. Two isolated production builds and app/admin CSS checks pass (437 Bootstrap variables each); a subsequent CSS-only production build preserves button focus contrast, and final JS/map revisions were copied/versioned through Mix's own tasks. Broader geography/guest/composer PHP checks ran 28 tests / 847 assertions with two pre-existing failures: Discover composer links still expect a removed `data-url` attribute, and My pieces still expects removed `Your folders` wording. Both reproduce in an isolated `git archive HEAD` baseline (2 tests / 12 assertions), outside this change. Browser checks at 1440×900 and 390×844 verify actual WebGL rendering, drag rotation, wheel zoom, continent/country switching, positive/zero counts, direct country picking, hover tooltips, native place selection, control contrast, a working country browse action, and no horizontal document overflow.
- **Remaining work / limits:** Not deployed. Physical-device pinch gestures and production MySQL/load behavior were not tested. Geography is generalized and counts reflect recorded country metadata, not birthplace or historical borders. No production performance or complete audit claim is made.

### 2026-10-10 — Block globe hover beneath portrait controls (P3, complete locally)

- **Request / evidence:** A country tooltip appeared behind composer portraits and their controls. Reproduced locally by hovering a France portrait: globe.gl continued raycasting the underlying country despite the existing click propagation guard.
- **Implementation / affected files:** `resources/js/views/composer-globe.js` suspends globe.gl pointer tracking on entry into the portrait group, immediately clearing its tooltip and suppressing underlying hover color/elevation while preserving the selected-country highlight. Leaving/cancelling, removing the group on zoom/selection changes, closing the modal and renderer disposal release the guard. Pending map clicks are ignored while over the group, and events from an old group cannot release a newer group's guard. `resources/sass/views/_composers.scss` enables pointer targeting across the entire group, including the country label and gaps. Rebuilt the controller, app CSS and their manifest entries; expanded the existing globe regression.
- **Compatibility:** Portrait links, paging, Close, globe movement outside the cards, cached geometry and URL state retain their behavior. No backend, mobile API, media, database or dependency changes. Preserved concurrent fullscreen-modal, MatchTour and portrait-style work.
- **Verification:** Focused globe JS passes with strict rejection handling; full JS regressions complete with existing unrelated mock-alert warnings. Checks cover blocked tracking, hover versus selection colors, pending map clicks, paging, leave/cancel, zoom removal, Close, modal closing and stale group events. Isolated production build and app/admin CSS-variable checks pass; source/output comparison and diff checks pass. Live desktop reproduction shows no tooltip while a portrait or country label is hovered and restores the Germany tooltip after leaving the group. At 390×844, paging works without an underlying tooltip and Close deselects successfully; normal map hover also works after closing portraits.
- **Remaining work / limits:** Not deployed; no physical touch-device testing.

### 2026-10-10 — Keep fullscreen gestures from zooming the page (P3, complete locally)

- **Request / evidence:** Extend the fullscreen score's double-tap/pinch page-zoom protection to the globe and every current/future shared fullscreen modal, while letting pinch gestures control globe zoom.
- **Implementation / affected files:** `resources/js/components/fullscreen-modals.js` adds one delegated, non-passive capture guard active only while a `.fullscreen-modal.show` exists. It cancels browser double-click, repeated-tap, multi-touch, Safari gesture and Ctrl-wheel zoom defaults without stopping propagation to content controls. Native single-finger scrolling, form focus/pickers, long presses, and button/link activation are retained; cancelled, closed and switched modal gestures reset the tap sequence. `resources/sass/components/_fullscreen-modal.scss` excludes browser pinch zoom on the shell and nested scrolling elements while allowing more specific content touch rules such as globe.gl's canvas `none`. Rebuilt app JS, the copied shared component, app CSS and their manifest entries; expanded `tests/js/fullscreen-modals.js`.
- **Compatibility:** Shared behavior automatically covers the globe, eScore, Find your match and future fullscreen shells. The existing fullscreen score implementation, normal-page behavior, viewport metadata, ordinary modals, mobile API/media contracts and database remain unchanged. Globe pointer/pinch handlers still receive gestures; no dependency changes.
- **Verification:** Focused shared-modal checks pass with strict rejection handling; full JS regressions, including the score pinch/scroll tests, complete with existing unrelated mock-alert warnings. Coverage includes all current/future shell IDs, default cancellation without propagation blocking, repeated control activation, disabled controls, native inputs, swipes, long presses, touch cancellation, modal switching and close/reopen. Isolated production build and app/admin CSS checks pass. A localhost-only fixture with the real shipped globe.gl confirms two-finger touch events remain delivered to content, Safari gesture defaults are cancelled, ordinary wheel scrolling remains uncancelled, and a synthetic Ctrl-wheel pinch changes globe altitude from 2.000 to 1.857 while its page-zoom default is cancelled. Closing the fixture modal restores the native wheel default. Live checks at 390×844 and 834×1112 confirm nested modal scrollers use `pan-x pan-y`, the globe canvas retains `none`, paging works, the viewport remains at scale 1, and no horizontal overflow occurs. Source/output comparison and diff checks pass.
- **Remaining work / limits:** Not deployed. Browser integration uses synthetic gesture events plus responsive layout checks; physical iPad/iPhone Safari pinch and double-tap behavior still requires device verification.

### 2026-10-10 — Give the globe a light default and retain its dark theme (P3, complete locally)

- **Request / evidence:** The navy globe modal felt out of place in the light app. The user wants a day appearance now and the current dark appearance preserved for future app-wide dark mode.
- **Implementation / affected files:** `resources/js/views/composer-globe.js` separates the existing dark palette from a new light palette with pale blue oceans, sage land and blue selection highlights. It follows the nearest `data-bs-theme` attribute on the modal or an ancestor, defaults to light, and observes theme changes without rebuilding country geometry or resetting the camera/selection. Set `data-bs-theme="dark"` on the app root when introducing global dark mode; removing the attribute restores light. `resources/sass/views/_composers.scss` keeps the original night styles and adds scoped light overrides for the shell, map, labels, portrait controls, tooltip and panel using app color tokens. The Blade legend now works with both palettes. Rebuilt app CSS, the globe controller and their manifest entries; expanded `tests/js/composer-globe.js`.
- **Compatibility:** Preserved the user's pending 16px country-label font change, globe zoom/rotation, highlight scope, portraits, loading state, URL restoration and fullscreen gesture protection. No backend, mobile API, media, database or dependency changes; no app-wide dark-mode control added.
- **Verification:** Focused globe regression passes with strict rejection handling; full JS regressions complete with existing unrelated mock-alert warnings. Tests cover original dark colors, light default independent of OS preference, inherited/local theme overrides, selection/URL/camera preservation, reuse of prepared geometry and closed-modal switching. Isolated production build and CSS checks pass. Live desktop and 390×844 checks confirm the light world/country views, portrait paging and no horizontal overflow. A localhost-only fixture with the shipped globe.gl, built controller and real map verifies day/night switching preserves the canvas and camera, and visually retains the navy/gold design. Source/output equality, manifest hashes and diff checks pass.
- **Remaining work / limits:** Not deployed. The future app-wide theme needs to use the documented attribute; mobile verification used a responsive browser rather than a physical device.
